diff --git a/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py b/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py
index 1ae3d60..b419b14 100644
--- a/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py
+++ b/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py
@@ -56,3 +56,98 @@ def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:
     if not has_completed_scan:
         print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
     return has_completed_scan
+
+
+def _saved_plan_review_ids(plan: dict | None) -> list[str]:
+    """Return review/concerns IDs recoverable from saved plan metadata."""
+    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
+        return []
+
+    ordered: list[str] = []
+    seen: set[str] = set()
+
+    def _append(issue_id: object) -> None:
+        if not isinstance(issue_id, str):
+            return
+        normalized = issue_id.strip()
+        if not normalized:
+            return
+        if not (
+            normalized.startswith("review::")
+            or normalized.startswith("concerns::")
+        ):
+            return
+        if normalized in seen:
+            return
+        seen.add(normalized)
+        ordered.append(normalized)
+
+    for issue_id in plan.get("queue_order", []):
+        _append(issue_id)
+
+    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
+    if isinstance(clusters, dict):
+        for cluster in clusters.values():
+            if not isinstance(cluster, dict):
+                continue
+            for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
+                _append(issue_id)
+            for step in cluster.get("action_steps", []):
+                if not isinstance(step, dict):
+                    continue
+                for issue_id in step.get("issue_refs", []):
+                    _append(issue_id)
+
+    return ordered
+
+
+def has_saved_plan_without_scan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> bool:
+    """Whether a saved plan can be resumed without a current scan state.
+
+    This is a narrow recovery path for plan/triage surfaces when ``plan.json``
+    still contains user-authored queue/cluster metadata but the matching
+    ``state-*.json`` file is gone.
+    """
+    if state.get("last_scan"):
+        return False
+    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
+        return False
+    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta")
+    triage_meta = meta if isinstance(meta, dict) else {}
+    return bool(
+        plan.get("queue_order")
+        or plan.get("clusters")
+        or triage_meta.get("triage_stages")
+        or triage_meta.get("strategy_summary")
+    )
+
+
+def recover_state_from_saved_plan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> dict:
+    """Hydrate placeholder review issues from a saved plan when scan state is gone."""
+    if not has_saved_plan_without_scan(state, plan):
+        return state
+
+    recovered = dict(state)
+    issues = state.get("issues", {})
+    recovered_issues = dict(issues) if isinstance(issues, dict) else {}
+
+    for issue_id in _saved_plan_review_ids(plan):
+        if issue_id in recovered_issues:
+            continue
+        parts = issue_id.split("::")
+        detector = "concerns" if issue_id.startswith("concerns::") else "review"
+        recovered_issues[issue_id] = {
+            "status": "open",
+            "detector": detector,
+            "file": parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else "",
+            "summary": issue_id,
+            "confidence": "medium",
+            "tier": 2,
+            "detail": {
+                "dimension": "unknown",
+                "recovered_from_plan": True,
+            },
+        }
+
+    recovered["issues"] = recovered_issues
+    return recovered
diff --git a/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py b/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py
index e960d34..a9de3dc 100644
--- a/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py
+++ b/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/helpers.py
@@ -235,6 +235,56 @@ def has_open_review_issues(state: StateModel | dict | None) -> bool:
     return bool(open_review_ids_from_state(state or {}))
 
 
+def cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
+    """Return the effective issue IDs for a cluster.
+
+    Some saved triage plans only retain issue membership via
+    ``action_steps[*].issue_refs`` after later workflow mutations clear
+    ``issue_ids``. Treat those refs as the durable cluster membership so
+    coverage and completion can continue without rebuilding the plan.
+    """
+    ordered: list[str] = []
+    seen: set[str] = set()
+
+    def _append(raw_ids: object) -> None:
+        if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
+            return
+        for raw_id in raw_ids:
+            if not isinstance(raw_id, str):
+                continue
+            issue_id = raw_id.strip()
+            if not issue_id or issue_id in seen:
+                continue
+            seen.add(issue_id)
+            ordered.append(issue_id)
+
+    _append(cluster.get("issue_ids"))
+
+    steps = cluster.get("action_steps")
+    if isinstance(steps, list):
+        for step in steps:
+            if not isinstance(step, dict):
+                continue
+            _append(step.get("issue_refs"))
+
+    return ordered
+
+
+def _cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
+    """Backward-compatible alias for internal imports."""
+    return cluster_issue_ids(cluster)
+
+
+def plan_review_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
+    """Return review/concerns IDs currently represented in queue_order."""
+    return [
+        fid for fid in _queue_order(plan)
+        if not fid.startswith("triage::")
+        and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
+        and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
+    ]
+
+

diff --git a/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py b/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..74cdcc7
--- /dev/null
+++ b/desloppify/engine/_state/recovery.py
@@ -0,0 +1,167 @@
+"""Recovery helpers for missing scan state with a surviving saved plan."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+
+_RECOVERY_MARKER = "_saved_plan_recovery"
+
+
+def _append_review_id(
+    ordered: list[str],
+    seen: set[str],
+    issue_id: object,
+) -> None:
+    if not isinstance(issue_id, str):
+        return
+    normalized = issue_id.strip()
+    if not normalized:
+        return
+    if not (
+        normalized.startswith("review::")
+        or normalized.startswith("concerns::")
+    ):
+        return
+    if normalized in seen:
+        return
+    seen.add(normalized)
+    ordered.append(normalized)
+
+
+def saved_plan_review_ids(
+    plan: dict | None,
+    *,
+    include_clusters: bool = True,
+) -> list[str]:
+    """Return review IDs recoverable from a saved plan.
+
+    When ``include_clusters`` is true, include IDs retained only in cluster
+    membership or ``action_steps[*].issue_refs``. This preserves the broader
+    compatibility contract used by manual recovery helpers.
+    """
+    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
+        return []
+
+    ordered: list[str] = []
+    seen: set[str] = set()
+
+    for issue_id in plan.get("queue_order", []):
+        _append_review_id(ordered, seen, issue_id)
+
+    if not include_clusters:
+        return ordered
+
+    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
+    if not isinstance(clusters, dict):
+        return ordered
+
+    for cluster in clusters.values():
+        if not isinstance(cluster, dict):
+            continue
+        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
+            _append_review_id(ordered, seen, issue_id)
+        for step in cluster.get("action_steps", []):
+            if not isinstance(step, dict):
+                continue
+            for issue_id in step.get("issue_refs", []):
+                _append_review_id(ordered, seen, issue_id)
+
+    return ordered
+
+
+def saved_plan_open_review_ids(plan: dict | None) -> list[str]:
+    """Return review IDs still represented in the current queue."""
+    return saved_plan_review_ids(plan, include_clusters=False)
+
+
+def has_saved_plan_without_scan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> bool:
+    """Whether a saved plan can be resumed without a current scan state."""
+    if state.get("last_scan"):
+        return False
+    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
+        return False
+    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta")
+    triage_meta = meta if isinstance(meta, dict) else {}
+    return bool(
+        plan.get("queue_order")
+        or plan.get("clusters")
+        or triage_meta.get("triage_stages")
+        or triage_meta.get("strategy_summary")
+    )
+
+
+def _hydrate_saved_issue_ids(
+    state: dict,
+    issue_ids: list[str],
+    *,
+    mode: str,
+) -> dict:
+    recovered = dict(state)
+    issues = state.get("issues", {})
+    recovered_issues = dict(issues) if isinstance(issues, dict) else {}
+
+    for issue_id in issue_ids:
+        if issue_id in recovered_issues:
+            continue
+        parts = issue_id.split("::")
+        detector = "concerns" if issue_id.startswith("concerns::") else "review"
+        recovered_issues[issue_id] = {
+            "id": issue_id,
+            "status": "open",
+            "detector": detector,
+            "file": parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else "",
+            "summary": issue_id,
+            "confidence": "medium",
+            "tier": 2,
+            "detail": {
+                "dimension": "unknown",
+                "recovered_from_plan": True,
+            },
+        }
+
+    recovered["issues"] = recovered_issues
+    recovered[_RECOVERY_MARKER] = {
+        "active": True,
+        "mode": mode,
+        "issue_count": len(issue_ids),
+    }
+    return recovered
+
+
+def recover_state_from_saved_plan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> dict:
+    """Hydrate all review IDs recoverable from a saved plan."""
+    if not has_saved_plan_without_scan(state, plan):
+        return state
+    return _hydrate_saved_issue_ids(
+        state,
+        saved_plan_review_ids(plan),
+        mode="all_saved_references",
+    )
+
+
+def recover_runtime_state_from_saved_plan(state: dict, plan: dict | None) -> dict:
+    """Hydrate only the review IDs still present in the live queue."""
+    if not has_saved_plan_without_scan(state, plan):
+        return state

> AGENT

Here's my honest assessment:

**Mixed. Some real improvements, some churn.**

**Genuinely good:**
- **State recovery from saved plans** (baf6075 + eb5976d) — Real feature work. When `state-*.json` is lost but `plan.json` survives, triage can resume instead of dying. The `recovery.py` module is well-structured with clear separation.
- **Label dedup** (c35883e) — `_CLUSTER_TYPE_LABELS` and `_ACTION_TYPE_LABELS` were duplicated between `queue_render.py` and `render_support.py`. Consolidating into `CLUSTER_TYPE_LABELS` is a clean win.
- **`cluster_issue_ids()` helper** — Handles the real problem of issue IDs living in `action_steps[*].issue_refs` after mutations clear `issue_ids`.

**Questionable / low-value:**
- **Subprocess "hardening"** (d472ea1 + 9411dd6) — This is mostly security theater. `shell=False` is already the default when you pass a list to `subprocess.run()`. Adding explicit `shell=False  # nosec B603` everywhere is just comment noise. The `_resolve_cli_executable(which(...))` pattern doesn't add meaningful security — it resolves to the same binary `PATH` lookup would find anyway. And that helper is copy-pasted into 4+ files rather than shared.
- **`QueueRenderContext` dataclass** (c35883e) — Replaces explicit keyword args with `render_context.state`, `render_context.opts`, etc. This trades one form of verbosity for another. The callers are no shorter (they still build the dataclass), and the callees are arguably harder to read (`render_context.state` vs `state`).
- **Churn between commits** — baf6075 adds `_saved_plan_review_ids` and `recover_state_from_saved_plan` to `helpers/state.py`, then eb5976d immediately moves nearly identical logic to a new `engine/_state/recovery.py`. That's two commits of churn for one outcome. The `_cluster_issue_ids` backward-compat alias is also dead weight given your no-backward-compat policy.

**Net: ~40% real improvement, ~60% ceremony.** The recovery feature is solid. The subprocess and render context changes added more code than they improved.

OK. I have a clear picture now. Here's my assessment:

---

## Uncommitted changes: Assessment

The current diff is **a genuine simplification**. It:

1. **Removes `is_saved_plan_recovery_state()`** — a function that checked a private `_saved_plan_recovery` marker. Replaced by `scan_metadata.source == "plan_reconstruction"` which is a cleaner, more canonical mechanism.
2. **Removes the `_saved_plan_recovery` dict** from hydrated state — the marker is now folded into `scan_metadata`, eliminating a parallel tracking structure.
3. **Simplifies `require_issue_inventory` / `require_completed_scan` / `require_scan_metrics`** — removes special-case branches that checked both `last_scan` and `is_saved_plan_recovery_state()`. Now they just delegate to `scan_inventory_available()` / `scan_metrics_available()`, which is cleaner since the schema module is the single source of truth.
4. **`require_completed_scan` becomes a thin alias** for `require_issue_inventory` — this is honest; it acknowledges they're the same check now.

**Verdict: This is good. Commit it.**

---

## Definitive plan for what to simplify/revert across recent commits

### 1. REVERT: Subprocess "hardening" (d472ea1 + 9411dd6) — **high priority**

These two commits are net negative. They:
- Add `shell=False` which is already the default (pure noise)
- Add `# nosec B603` / `# nosec B404` comments that are Bandit-suppression boilerplate
- Copy-paste `_resolve_cli_executable` into **8 files** (a new smell introduced while supposedly fixing smells)
- `which("git") or "git"` doesn't add security — same PATH lookup

**Action:** Revert both commits entirely. If you want Bandit suppression, add a project-level `.bandit` config or `pyproject.toml` `[tool.bandit]` exclusion instead of per-line comments.

### 2. SIMPLIFY: Dead aliases — **quick win**

- `_saved_plan_review_ids()` in `helpers/state.py:83` — just calls `saved_plan_review_ids()`, no callers. Delete it + remove from `__all__`.
- `_cluster_issue_ids()` alias in `triage/helpers.py:246` — wraps `cluster_issue_ids()`, only called internally within that same file. Inline or remove.

### 3. SIMPLIFY: `QueueRenderContext` dataclass (from c35883e) — **medium priority**

This replaced explicit kwargs with `render_context.queue`, `render_context.state`, etc. The callsites aren't shorter and the callees are harder to read. Two options:
- **Option A (preferred):** Revert the dataclass, keep the label dedup (which was the good part of that commit).
- **Option B:** If you want to keep it, at least destructure at the top of each function: `state = render_context.state` etc., so the body reads naturally.

### 4. KEEP: State recovery feature (baf6075 + eb5976d)

The core recovery logic is real and useful. The current uncommitted changes improve it further by consolidating on `scan_metadata`. **Commit the current changes, then clean up:**
- After committing, verify `_saved_plan_recovery` marker is fully gone from all code/tests
- The `_cluster_issue_ids` backward-compat alias in `triage/helpers.py` should go (per your no-backward-compat policy)

### 5. KEEP: Label dedup from c35883e

Moving `_CLUSTER_TYPE_LABELS` / `_ACTION_TYPE_LABELS` to a shared `CLUSTER_TYPE_LABELS` is fine. The `ClusterTypeLabels` frozen dataclass is borderline (a plain dict would work), but it's small and not hurting anything.

---

### Summary

| Item | Action | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Subprocess hardening (2 commits) | **Revert** | Security theater, 8x copy-paste |
| `_saved_plan_review_ids` alias | **Delete** | Zero callers |
| `_cluster_issue_ids` alias | **Delete** | No-compat policy |
| `QueueRenderContext` | **Revert** (keep label dedup) | Net-negative readability |
| Current uncommitted changes | **Commit** | Genuine simplification |
| State recovery feature | **Keep** | Real value |
| Label dedup | **Keep** | Real dedup |

1→"""Read-only git observation with graceful degradation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import subprocess  # nosec B404
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass
     8→from shutil import which
     9→
    10→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    11→
    12→_GIT_TIMEOUT = 5
    13→
    14→
    15→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    16→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    17→    return which(name) or name
    18→
    19→
    20→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    21→class GitContext:
    22→    available: bool
    23→    branch: str | None = None
    24→    head_sha: str | None = None
    25→    has_uncommitted: bool = False
    26→    root: str | None = None
    27→
    28→
    29→def detect_git_context() -> GitContext:
    30→    """Detect current git context (branch, HEAD, uncommitted changes).
    31→
    32→    Returns ``available=False`` when git is missing or not in a repo.
    33→    """
    34→    git_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("git")
    35→    try:
    36→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    37→        head = subprocess.run(
    38→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "HEAD"],
    39→            capture_output=True,
    40→            text=True,
    41→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    42→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    43→        )
    44→        if head.returncode != 0:
    45→            return GitContext(available=False)
    46→
    47→        sha = head.stdout.strip()[:12]
    48→
    49→        branch_result = subprocess.run(
    50→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "--abbrev-ref", "HEAD"],
    51→            capture_output=True,
    52→            text=True,
    53→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    54→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    55→        )
    56→        branch = branch_result.stdout.strip() if branch_result.returncode == 0 else None
    57→
    58→        root_result = subprocess.run(
    59→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
    60→            capture_output=True,
    61→            text=True,
    62→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    63→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    64→        )
    65→        root = root_result.stdout.strip() if root_result.returncode == 0 else None
    66→
    67→        status_result = subprocess.run(
    68→            [git_cli, "status", "--porcelain"],
    69→            capture_output=True,
    70→            text=True,
    71→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    72→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    73→        )
    74→        has_uncommitted = bool(status_result.stdout.strip()) if status_result.returncode == 0 else False
    75→
    76→        return GitContext(
    77→            available=True,
    78→            branch=branch,
    79→            head_sha=sha,
    80→            has_uncommitted=has_uncommitted,
    81→            root=root,
    82→        )
    83→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError) as exc:
    84→        logger.debug("git context unavailable: %s", exc)
    85→        return GitContext(available=False)
    86→
    87→
    88→def update_pr_body(pr_number: int, body: str) -> bool:
    89→    """Update PR description via ``gh pr edit``.  Returns True on success."""
    90→    gh_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("gh")
    91→    try:
    92→        # Static gh argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    93→        result = subprocess.run(
    94→            [gh_cli, "pr", "edit", str(pr_number), "--body", body],
    95→            capture_output=True,
    96→            text=True,
    97→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT * 3,
    98→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    99→        )
   100→        if result.returncode != 0:
   101→            logger.warning("gh pr edit failed: %s", result.stderr.strip())
   102→            return False
   103→        return True
   104→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError) as exc:
   105→        logger.warning("gh pr edit unavailable: %s", exc)
   106→        return False
   107→
   108→
   109→__all__ = ["GitContext", "detect_git_context", "update_pr_body"]
   110→

1→"""Metadata helpers for scorecard rendering."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import re
     7→import subprocess  # nosec B404
     8→from collections.abc import Callable
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from shutil import which
    11→
    12→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→
    17→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    18→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    19→    return which(name) or name
    20→
    21→
    22→def resolve_project_name(project_root: Path) -> str:
    23→    """Resolve owner/repo display name from GitHub CLI, git remote, or folder."""
    24→    gh_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("gh")
    25→    try:
    26→        # Static gh argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    27→        name = subprocess.check_output(
    28→            [
    29→                gh_cli,
    30→                "repo",
    31→                "view",
    32→                "--json",
    33→                "nameWithOwner",
    34→                "-q",
    35→                ".nameWithOwner",
    36→            ],
    37→            cwd=str(project_root),
    38→            stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL,
    39→            text=True,
    40→            timeout=5,
    41→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    42→        ).strip()
    43→        if "/" in name:
    44→            return name
    45→    except (
    46→        subprocess.CalledProcessError,
    47→        FileNotFoundError,
    48→        subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
    49→    ) as exc:
    50→        logger.debug("gh repo view failed, falling back to git remote: %s", exc)
    51→
    52→    try:
    53→        git_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("git")
    54→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    55→        url = subprocess.check_output(
    56→            [git_cli, "config", "--get", "remote.origin.url"],
    57→            cwd=str(project_root),
    58→            stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL,
    59→            text=True,
    60→            timeout=5,
    61→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    62→        ).strip()
    63→        if url.startswith("git@") and ":" in url:
    64→            path = url.split(":")[-1]
    65→        else:
    66→            path = "/".join(url.split("/")[-2:])
    67→        return path.removesuffix(".git")
    68→    except (
    69→        subprocess.CalledProcessError,
    70→        FileNotFoundError,
    71→        IndexError,
    72→        subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
    73→    ) as exc:
    74→        logger.debug("git remote lookup failed, falling back to folder name: %s", exc)
    75→        return project_root.name
    76→
    77→
    78→def resolve_package_version(
    79→    project_root: Path,
    80→    *,
    81→    version_getter: Callable[[str], str],
    82→    package_not_found_error: type[Exception],
    83→) -> str:
    84→    """Resolve package version from installed metadata or local pyproject."""
    85→    try:
    86→        return version_getter("desloppify")
    87→    except package_not_found_error as exc:
    88→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read package metadata version", exc)
    89→
    90→    pyproject_path = project_root / "pyproject.toml"
    91→    try:
    92→        text = pyproject_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    93→        match = re.search(r'^\s*version\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"\s*$', text, re.MULTILINE)
    94→        if match:
    95→            return match.group(1)
    96→    except OSError as exc:
    97→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read pyproject.toml for package version", exc)
    98→
    99→    return "unknown"
   100→

1→"""Retro/checklist and git-safety helpers for autofix apply flow."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import subprocess  # nosec B404
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→from shutil import which
     9→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    12→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    13→from desloppify.languages.framework import FixResult
    14→
    15→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    16→    from desloppify.languages.framework import LangRun
    17→
    18→
    19→def _resolve_fixer_results(
    20→    state: dict, results: list[dict], detector: str, fixer_name: str
    21→) -> list[str]:
    22→    resolved_ids = []
    23→    for result in results:
    24→        result_file = rel(result["file"])
    25→        for symbol in result["removed"]:
    26→            issue_id = f"{detector}::{result_file}::{symbol}"
    27→            if issue_id in state["issues"] and state["issues"][issue_id]["status"] == "open":
    28→                state["issues"][issue_id]["status"] = "fixed"
    29→                state["issues"][issue_id]["note"] = (
    30→                    f"auto-fixed by desloppify autofix {fixer_name}"
    31→                )
    32→                resolved_ids.append(issue_id)
    33→    return resolved_ids
    34→
    35→
    36→def _warn_uncommitted_changes() -> None:
    37→    git_executable = which("git") or "git"
    38→    try:
    39→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion or user-provided executable path.
    40→        result = subprocess.run(
    41→            [git_executable, "status", "--porcelain"],
    42→            capture_output=True,
    43→            text=True,
    44→            timeout=5,
    45→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    46→        )
    47→        if result.stdout.strip():
    48→            print(colorize("\n  ⚠ You have uncommitted changes. Consider running:", "yellow"))
    49→            print(
    50→                colorize(
    51→                    "    git add -A && git commit -m 'pre-fix checkpoint' && git push",
    52→                    "yellow",
    53→                )
    54→            )
    55→            print(
    56→                colorize(
    57→                    "    This ensures you can revert if the fixer produces unexpected results.\n",
    58→                    "dim",
    59→                )
    60→            )
    61→    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
    62→        return
    63→
    64→
    65→def _cascade_unused_import_cleanup(
    66→    path: Path,
    67→    state: dict,
    68→    _prev_score: float,
    69→    dry_run: bool,
    70→    *,
    71→    lang: LangRun | None = None,
    72→) -> None:
    73→    if not lang or "unused-imports" not in getattr(lang, "fixers", {}):
    74→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no unused-imports fixer for this language", "dim"))
    75→        return
    76→
    77→    fixer = lang.fixers["unused-imports"]
    78→    print(colorize("\n  Running cascading import cleanup...", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    79→    entries = fixer.detect(path)
    80→    if not entries:
    81→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    raw_results = fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
    85→    results = raw_results.entries if isinstance(raw_results, FixResult) else raw_results
    86→
    87→    if not results:
    88→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
    89→        return
    90→
    91→    removed_count = sum(len(result["removed"]) for result in results)
    92→    removed_lines = sum(result["lines_removed"] for result in results)
    93→    print(
    94→        colorize(
    95→            f"  Cascade: removed {removed_count} now-orphaned imports "
    96→            f"from {len(results)} files ({removed_lines} lines)",
    97→            "green",
    98→        )
    99→    )
   100→    resolved = _resolve_fixer_results(
   101→        state, results, fixer.detector, "cascade-unused-imports"
   102→    )
   103→    if resolved:
   104→        print(f"  Cascade: auto-resolved {len(resolved)} import issues")
   105→
   106→
   107→_SKIP_REASON_LABELS = {
   108→    "rest_element": "has ...rest (removing changes rest contents)",
   109→    "array_destructuring": "array destructuring (positional — can't remove)",
   110→    "function_param": "function/callback parameter (use `fix unused-params` to prefix with _)",
   111→    "standalone_var_with_call": "standalone variable with function call (may have side effects)",
   112→    "no_destr_context": "destructuring member without context",
   113→    "out_of_range": "line out of range (stale data?)",
   114→    "other": "other patterns (needs manual review)",
   115→}
   116→
   117→
   118→def _print_fix_retro(
   119→    fixer_name: str,
   120→    detected: int,
   121→    fixed: int,
   122→    resolved: int,
   123→    skip_reasons: dict[str, int] | None = None,
   124→):
   125→    skipped = detected - fixed
   126→    print(colorize("\n  ── Post-fix check ──", "dim"))
   127→    print(
   128→        colorize(
   129→            f"  Fixed {fixed}/{detected} ({skipped} skipped, {resolved} issues resolved)",
   130→            "dim",
   131→        )
   132→    )
   133→    if skip_reasons and skipped > 0:
   134→        print(colorize(f"\n  Skip reasons ({skipped} total):", "dim"))
   135→        for reason, count in sorted(skip_reasons.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1]):
   136→            print(
   137→                colorize(
   138→                    f"    {count:4d}  {_SKIP_REASON_LABELS.get(reason, reason)}", "dim"
   139→                )
   140→            )
   141→        print()
   142→
   143→    checklist = [
   144→        "Run your language typecheck/build command — does it still build?",
   145→        "Spot-check a few changed files — do the edits look correct?",
   146→    ]
   147→    if skipped > 0 and not skip_reasons:
   148→        checklist.append(
   149→            f"{skipped} items were skipped. Should the fixer handle more patterns?"
   150→        )
   151→    checklist += [
   152→        "Run `desloppify scan` to update state and refresh issues.",
   153→        "Are there cascading effects? (e.g., removing vars may orphan imports)",
   154→        "`git diff --stat` — review before committing. Anything surprising?",
   155→    ]
   156→    print(colorize("  Checklist:", "dim"))
   157→    for index, item in enumerate(checklist, 1):
   158→        print(colorize(f"  {index}. {item}", "dim"))
   159→
   160→
   161→__all__ = [
   162→    "_cascade_unused_import_cleanup",
   163→    "_print_fix_retro",
   164→    "_resolve_fixer_results",
   165→    "_warn_uncommitted_changes",
   166→]
   167→

1→"""Detect repeated code blocks via jscpd.
     2→
     3→Replaces boilerplate_duplication.py with a thin adapter around jscpd
     4→(https://github.com/kucherenko/jscpd), which uses proper per-language
     5→tokenisation and supports type-2 clones (renamed identifiers).
     6→
     7→Falls back gracefully to None when jscpd/npx is not installed.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from __future__ import annotations
    11→
    12→import hashlib
    13→import json
    14→import logging
    15→import shutil
    16→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    17→import tempfile
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→
    20→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import (
    21→
    22→    collect_exclude_dirs,
    23→
    24→    get_exclusions,
    25→
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import warn_best_effort
    28→
    29→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    30→
    31→_BASE_IGNORES = (
    32→    "**/node_modules/**",
    33→    "**/.git/**",
    34→    "**/__pycache__/**",
    35→    "**/.venv*/**",
    36→    "**/venv/**",
    37→    "**/.desloppify/**",
    38→    "**/.claude/**",
    39→)
    40→
    41→_ARTIFACT_PREFIXES = ("build/", "dist/", ".desloppify/", ".claude/")
    42→_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX = "build/lib/"
    43→
    44→
    45→def _to_scan_relative(path_resolved: Path, name: str) -> str | None:
    46→    """Return scan-relative path, or None when file is outside scan path."""
    47→    if not name:
    48→        return None
    49→    try:
    50→        rel = Path(name).resolve().relative_to(path_resolved)
    51→    except (ValueError, OSError):
    52→        return None
    53→    return str(rel).replace("\\", "/")
    54→
    55→
    56→def _is_artifact_path(rel_path: str) -> bool:
    57→    return rel_path.startswith(_ARTIFACT_PREFIXES)
    58→
    59→
    60→def _is_build_mirror_pair(first_rel: str, second_rel: str) -> bool:
    61→    if first_rel.startswith(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):
    62→        return first_rel[len(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):] == second_rel
    63→    if second_rel.startswith(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):
    64→        return second_rel[len(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):] == first_rel
    65→    return False
    66→
    67→
    68→def _as_jscpd_globs(pattern: str) -> list[str]:
    69→    raw = pattern.strip().replace("\\", "/").strip("/")
    70→    if not raw:
    71→        return []
    72→    globs = [f"**/{raw}/**"]
    73→    basename = raw.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
    74→    if "." in basename or "*" in basename:
    75→        globs.append(f"**/{raw}")
    76→    return globs
    77→
    78→
    79→def _jscpd_ignore_arg(scan_path: Path) -> str:
    80→    """Build jscpd ignore globs from defaults + runtime excludes."""
    81→    patterns = set(_BASE_IGNORES)
    82→    # Use collect_exclude_dirs for the directory-name portion, then convert
    83→    # the remaining runtime globs (patterns with '*') to jscpd format.
    84→    for dirname in collect_exclude_dirs(scan_path):
    85→        base = Path(dirname).name
    86→        patterns.update(_as_jscpd_globs(base))
    87→    for pattern in get_exclusions():
    88→        if "*" in pattern:
    89→            patterns.update(_as_jscpd_globs(pattern))
    90→    return ",".join(sorted(patterns))
    91→
    92→
    93→def _parse_jscpd_report(report: dict, scan_path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    94→    """Parse a jscpd JSON report dict and return clustered duplication entries.
    95→
    96→    Clusters pairwise duplicates that share the same fragment into multi-file
    97→    groups, producing the same output shape as the old boilerplate_duplication
    98→    detector so that phase_boilerplate_duplication() needs no further changes.
    99→    """
   100→    duplicates = report.get("duplicates", [])
   101→    if not duplicates:
   102→        return []
   103→
   104→    path_resolved = scan_path.resolve()
   105→
   106→    # Cluster pairs by SHA256(fragment.strip()[:200])[:16]
   107→    clusters: dict[str, dict] = {}
   108→
   109→    for dup in duplicates:
   110→        fragment = dup.get("fragment", "")
   111→        fragment_key = hashlib.sha256(
   112→            fragment.strip()[:200].encode("utf-8", errors="replace")
   113→        ).hexdigest()[:16]
   114→
   115→        first = dup.get("firstFile", {})
   116→        second = dup.get("secondFile", {})
   117→        first_name = first.get("name", "")
   118→        second_name = second.get("name", "")
   119→
   120→        first_rel = _to_scan_relative(path_resolved, first_name)
   121→        second_rel = _to_scan_relative(path_resolved, second_name)
   122→        if first_rel is None or second_rel is None:
   123→            continue
   124→        if (
   125→            first_rel == second_rel
   126→            or _is_artifact_path(first_rel)
   127→            or _is_artifact_path(second_rel)
   128→            or _is_build_mirror_pair(first_rel, second_rel)
   129→        ):
   130→            continue
   131→
   132→        lines = dup.get("lines", 0)
   133→        if fragment_key not in clusters:
   134→            clusters[fragment_key] = {
   135→                "id": fragment_key,
   136→                "lines": lines,
   137→                "fragment": fragment,
   138→                "files": {},
   139→            }
   140→
   141→        cluster = clusters[fragment_key]
   142→        for name, info in [(first_rel, first), (second_rel, second)]:
   143→            if name not in cluster["files"]:
   144→                cluster["files"][name] = info.get("start", 0)
   145→
   146→    entries: list[dict] = []
   147→    for cluster in clusters.values():
   148→        files = cluster["files"]
   149→        if len(files) < 2:
   150→            continue
   151→        locations = [
   152→            {"file": f, "line": line}
   153→            for f, line in sorted(files.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[0])
   154→        ]
   155→        entries.append(
   156→            {
   157→                "id": cluster["id"],
   158→                "distinct_files": len(files),
   159→                "window_size": cluster["lines"],
   160→                "locations": locations,
   161→                "sample": cluster["fragment"].splitlines()[:4],
   162→            }
   163→        )
   164→
   165→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["distinct_files"], e["id"]))
   166→    return entries
   167→
   168→
   169→def _resolve_jscpd_command() -> list[str] | None:
   170→    """Return the best available command prefix for running jscpd.
   171→
   172→    Prefers a locally/globally installed ``jscpd`` executable over ``npx``.
   173→    Returns ``None`` when neither ``jscpd`` nor ``npx`` can be found.
   174→    """
   175→    jscpd_path = shutil.which("jscpd")
   176→    if jscpd_path:
   177→        logger.debug("jscpd: using installed executable at %s", jscpd_path)
   178→        return [jscpd_path]
   179→    npx_path = shutil.which("npx")
   180→    if npx_path:
   181→        logger.debug("jscpd: falling back to npx at %s", npx_path)
   182→        return [npx_path, "--yes", "jscpd"]
   183→    return None
   184→
   185→
   186→def detect_with_jscpd(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
   187→    """Run jscpd on *path* and return duplication entries, or None on failure."""
   188→    cmd_prefix = _resolve_jscpd_command()
   189→    if cmd_prefix is None:
   190→        warn_best_effort(
   191→            "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd/npx not found. "
   192→            "Install with `npm install -g jscpd`."
   193→        )
   194→        logger.debug("jscpd: neither jscpd nor npx found — skipping")
   195→        return None
   196→
   197→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   198→        try:
   199→            # Static jscpd argv only; command prefix is resolved before execution.
   200→            subprocess.run(
   201→                [
   202→                    *cmd_prefix,
   203→                    str(path),
   204→                    "--reporters",
   205→                    "json",
   206→                    "--output",
   207→                    tmpdir,
   208→                    "--min-lines",
   209→                    "4",
   210→                    "--min-tokens",
   211→                    "50",
   212→                    "--ignore",
   213→                    _jscpd_ignore_arg(path),
   214→                    "--silent",
   215→                ],
   216→                capture_output=True,
   217→                text=True,
   218→                timeout=120,
   219→                check=True,
   220→                shell=False,  # nosec B603
   221→            )
   222→        except FileNotFoundError:
   223→            warn_best_effort(
   224→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd/npx not found. "
   225→                "Install with `npm install -g jscpd`."
   226→            )
   227→            logger.debug("jscpd: command not found at runtime — skipping")
   228→            return None
   229→        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as exc:
   230→            warn_best_effort(
   231→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd exited with errors."
   232→            )
   233→            logger.debug(
   234→                "jscpd: non-zero exit (%s): %s",
   235→                exc.returncode,
   236→                (exc.stderr or "").strip(),
   237→            )
   238→            return None
   239→        except OSError as exc:
   240→            warn_best_effort(
   241→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd failed to run."
   242→            )
   243→            logger.debug("jscpd: OS error running jscpd: %s", exc)
   244→            return None
   245→        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   246→            warn_best_effort(
   247→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd timed out after 120s."
   248→            )
   249→            logger.debug("jscpd: timed out")
   250→            return None
   251→
   252→        report_file = Path(tmpdir) / "jscpd-report.json"
   253→        if not report_file.exists():
   254→            logger.debug("jscpd: no report file produced — assuming no duplicates")
   255→            return []
   256→
   257→        try:
   258→            report = json.loads(report_file.read_text())
   259→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   260→            logger.debug("jscpd: failed to parse report: %s", exc)
   261→            return None
   262→
   263→        return _parse_jscpd_report(report, path)
   264→
   265→
   266→__all__ = ["_parse_jscpd_report", "_resolve_jscpd_command", "detect_with_jscpd"]
   267→

1→"""Detect architectural layer violations via import-linter.
     2→
     3→Reads `.importlinter` config from the scanned project's root (walking up
     4→from the scan path to find the first `.importlinter` file, stopping at `.git`
     5→boundaries). Falls back gracefully to None when lint-imports is not installed
     6→or no config is found.
     7→
     8→See https://import-linter.readthedocs.io for config format.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import re
    15→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    16→from pathlib import Path
    17→from shutil import which
    18→
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→_BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE = re.compile(r"Broken contract '([^']+)'")
    22→# import-linter outputs violations as "  module.a imports module.b"
    23→_VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+imports\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    24→# Also handle "  module.a -> module.b" format (arrow notation)
    25→_VIOLATION_ARROW_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+->\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    26→
    27→
    28→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    29→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    30→    return which(name) or name
    31→
    32→
    33→def _find_importlinter_root(path: Path) -> Path | None:
    34→    """Walk up from path to find the directory containing .importlinter.
    35→
    36→    Stops when a .git boundary or the filesystem root is encountered.
    37→    """
    38→    candidate = path.resolve()
    39→    for directory in [candidate, *candidate.parents]:
    40→        if (directory / ".importlinter").exists():
    41→            return directory
    42→        # Stop at project root boundaries
    43→        if (directory / ".git").exists() or directory.parent == directory:
    44→            break
    45→    return None
    46→
    47→
    48→def _module_to_file(module: str) -> str:
    49→    """Convert dotted module name to a relative file path."""
    50→    return module.replace(".", "/") + ".py"
    51→
    52→
    53→def detect_with_import_linter(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    54→    """Run lint-imports and return layer violation entries, or None on failure."""
    55→    config_dir = _find_importlinter_root(path)
    56→    if config_dir is None:
    57→        logger.debug("import-linter: no .importlinter config found — skipping")
    58→        return None
    59→
    60→    try:
    61→        # Static lint-imports argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    62→        result = subprocess.run(
    63→            [_resolve_cli_executable("lint-imports")],
    64→            capture_output=True,
    65→            text=True,
    66→            cwd=config_dir,
    67→            timeout=60,
    68→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    69→        )
    70→    except FileNotFoundError:
    71→        logger.debug("import-linter: lint-imports not found — skipping")
    72→        return None
    73→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    74→        logger.debug("import-linter: timed out")
    75→        return None
    76→
    77→    if result.returncode == 0:
    78→        return []
    79→
    80→    output = result.stdout + result.stderr
    81→    if not output.strip():
    82→        return []
    83→
    84→    entries: list[dict] = []
    85→    current_contract: str | None = None
    86→
    87→    for line in output.splitlines():
    88→        m_contract = _BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE.search(line)
    89→        if m_contract:
    90→            current_contract = m_contract.group(1)
    91→            continue
    92→
    93→        if current_contract is None:
    94→            continue
    95→
    96→        m_viol = _VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE.match(line) or _VIOLATION_ARROW_RE.match(line)
    97→        if not m_viol:
    98→            continue
    99→
   100→        source_mod = m_viol.group(1)
   101→        target_mod = m_viol.group(2)
   102→        source_file = _module_to_file(source_mod)
   103→        source_pkg = source_mod.rsplit(".", 1)[-1] if "." in source_mod else source_mod
   104→
   105→        entries.append(
   106→            {
   107→                "file": source_file,
   108→                "line": 0,
   109→                "summary": (
   110→                    f"Layer violation: {source_mod} -> {target_mod}"
   111→                    f" (contract: {current_contract})"
   112→                ),
   113→                "confidence": "high",
   114→                "source_pkg": source_pkg,
   115→                "target_pkg": target_mod,
   116→            }
   117→        )
   118→
   119→    return entries
   120→
   121→
   122→__all__ = ["detect_with_import_linter"]
   123→

1→"""Python unused detection via ruff (F401=unused imports, F841=unused vars)."""
     2→
     3→import json
     4→import re
     5→import subprocess  # nosec B404
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from shutil import which
     8→
     9→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import collect_exclude_dirs as _collect_exclude_dirs
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_py_files
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import get_exclusions as _get_exclusions
    12→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion as _matches_exclusion
    13→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    14→
    15→
    16→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    17→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    18→    return which(name) or name
    19→
    20→
    21→def _selected_codes(category: str) -> list[str]:
    22→    select = []
    23→    if category in ("all", "imports"):
    24→        select.append("F401")
    25→    if category in ("all", "vars"):
    26→        select.append("F841")
    27→    return select
    28→
    29→
    30→def _is_excluded(filepath: str, exclusions: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
    31→    return bool(
    32→        exclusions
    33→        and any(_matches_exclusion(filepath, ex) for ex in exclusions)
    34→    )
    35→
    36→
    37→def _extract_unused_name(message: str, *, name_re: re.Pattern[str]) -> str:
    38→    match = name_re.search(message)
    39→    return match.group(1) if match else message.split()[0]
    40→
    41→
    42→def _parse_ruff_diagnostics(
    43→    diagnostics: list[dict],
    44→    *,
    45→    category: str,
    46→    exclusions: tuple[str, ...],
    47→) -> list[dict]:
    48→    entries = []
    49→    name_re = re.compile(r"`([^`]+)`")
    50→    for diagnostic in diagnostics:
    51→        code = diagnostic.get("code", "")
    52→        filepath = diagnostic.get("filename", "")
    53→        if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
    54→            continue
    55→
    56→        # F401 in __init__.py = re-export, not dead code
    57→        if code == "F401" and filepath.endswith("__init__.py"):
    58→            continue
    59→
    60→        cat = "imports" if code == "F401" else "vars"
    61→        if category != "all" and cat != category:
    62→            continue
    63→
    64→        message = diagnostic.get("message", "")
    65→        name = _extract_unused_name(message, name_re=name_re)
    66→        if name.startswith("_"):
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        location = diagnostic.get("location", {})
    70→        entries.append(
    71→            {
    72→                "file": filepath,
    73→                "line": location.get("row", 0),
    74→                "col": location.get("column", 0),
    75→                "name": name,
    76→                "category": cat,
    77→            }
    78→        )
    79→    return entries
    80→
    81→
    82→def _parse_pyflakes_lines(
    83→    lines: list[str],
    84→    *,
    85→    category: str,
    86→    exclusions: tuple[str, ...],
    87→) -> list[dict]:
    88→    entries = []
    89→    import_re = re.compile(r"^(.+):(\d+):\d*\s+'([^']+)'\s+imported but unused")
    90→    var_re = re.compile(
    91→        r"^(.+):(\d+):\d*\s+local variable '([^']+)' is assigned to but never used"
    92→    )
    93→
    94→    for line in lines:
    95→        import_match = import_re.match(line)
    96→        if import_match and category in ("all", "imports"):
    97→            filepath = import_match.group(1)
    98→            if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
    99→                continue
   100→            # F401 in __init__.py = re-export, not dead code
   101→            if filepath.endswith("__init__.py"):
   102→                continue
   103→            entries.append(
   104→                {
   105→                    "file": filepath,
   106→                    "line": int(import_match.group(2)),
   107→                    "col": 0,
   108→                    "name": import_match.group(3),
   109→                    "category": "imports",
   110→                }
   111→            )
   112→            continue
   113→
   114→        var_match = var_re.match(line)
   115→        if var_match and category in ("all", "vars"):
   116→            filepath = var_match.group(1)
   117→            if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
   118→                continue
   119→            entries.append(
   120→                {
   121→                    "file": filepath,
   122→                    "line": int(var_match.group(2)),
   123→                    "col": 0,
   124→                    "name": var_match.group(3),
   125→                    "category": "vars",
   126→                }
   127→            )
   128→    return entries
   129→
   130→
   131→def detect_unused(path: Path, category: str = "all") -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   132→    """Detect unused imports and variables using ruff.
   133→
   134→    Falls back to pyflakes if ruff is not available.
   135→    Returns (entries, total_statements_checked).
   136→    """
   137→    total_files = len(find_py_files(path))
   138→
   139→    entries = _try_ruff(path, category)
   140→    if entries is not None:
   141→        return entries, total_files
   142→
   143→    entries = _try_pyflakes(path, category)
   144→    if entries is not None:
   145→        return entries, total_files
   146→
   147→    return [], total_files
   148→
   149→
   150→def _try_ruff(path: Path, category: str) -> list[dict] | None:
   151→    """Try ruff for unused detection."""
   152→    select = _selected_codes(category)
   153→    if not select:
   154→        return []
   155→
   156→    exclude_dirs = _collect_exclude_dirs(path)
   157→    cmd = [
   158→        "ruff",
   159→        "check",
   160→        "--select",
   161→        ",".join(select),
   162→        "--output-format",
   163→        "json",
   164→        "--no-fix",
   165→    ]
   166→    if exclude_dirs:
   167→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
   168→    cmd.append(str(path))
   169→
   170→    try:
   171→        # Static ruff argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   172→        result = subprocess.run(
   173→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
   174→            capture_output=True,
   175→            text=True,
   176→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   177→            timeout=60,
   178→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   179→        )
   180→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   181→        return None
   182→
   183→    if not result.stdout.strip():
   184→        return []
   185→
   186→    try:
   187→        diagnostics = json.loads(result.stdout)
   188→    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   189→        return None
   190→
   191→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   192→    return _parse_ruff_diagnostics(
   193→        diagnostics, category=category, exclusions=exclusions
   194→    )
   195→
   196→
   197→def _try_pyflakes(path: Path, category: str) -> list[dict] | None:
   198→    """Fallback: try pyflakes for unused detection."""
   199→    try:
   200→        # Static pyflakes argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   201→        result = subprocess.run(
   202→            [_resolve_cli_executable("pyflakes"), str(path)],
   203→            capture_output=True,
   204→            text=True,
   205→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   206→            timeout=60,
   207→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   208→        )
   209→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   210→        return None
   211→
   212→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   213→    lines = (result.stdout + result.stderr).splitlines()
   214→    return _parse_pyflakes_lines(lines, category=category, exclusions=exclusions)
   215→

1→"""Bandit adapter — Python security detection via the bandit static analyser.
     2→
     3→Runs ``bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>`` as a subprocess and converts its JSON
     4→output into the security entry dicts expected by ``phase_security``.
     5→
     6→Bandit covers AST-level security checks (shell injection, unsafe deserialization,
     7→SQL injection, etc.) more reliably than custom regex/AST patterns. When bandit is
     8→installed, it is used as the lang-specific security detector; otherwise
     9→Python-specific security checks will be skipped.
    10→
    11→Bandit severity → desloppify tier/confidence mapping:
    12→  HIGH   → tier=4, confidence="high"
    13→  MEDIUM → tier=3, confidence="medium"
    14→  LOW    → tier=3, confidence="low"
    15→
    16→The ``check_id`` in the entry detail is the bandit test ID (e.g., "B602") so
    17→issues are stable across reruns and can be wontfix-tracked by ID.
    18→"""
    19→
    20→from __future__ import annotations
    21→
    22→import json
    23→import logging
    24→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    25→from dataclasses import dataclass
    26→from pathlib import Path
    27→from shutil import which
    28→from typing import Literal
    29→
    30→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    31→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    32→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap, Zone
    33→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import DetectorCoverageStatus
    34→
    35→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    36→
    37→_SEVERITY_TO_TIER = {"HIGH": 4, "MEDIUM": 3, "LOW": 3}
    38→_SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE = {"HIGH": "high", "MEDIUM": "medium", "LOW": "low"}
    39→
    40→# Bandit test IDs that overlap with the cross-language security detector
    41→# (secret names, hardcoded passwords). Skip these to avoid duplicate issues.
    42→_CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP = frozenset(
    43→    {
    44→        "B105",  # hardcoded_password_string
    45→        "B106",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    46→        "B107",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    47→        "B501",  # request_with_no_cert_validation  (covered by weak_crypto_tls)
    48→        "B502",  # ssl_with_bad_version
    49→        "B503",  # ssl_with_bad_defaults
    50→        "B504",  # ssl_with_no_version
    51→        "B505",  # weak_cryptographic_key
    52→    }
    53→)
    54→
    55→_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT = (
    56→    "Python-specific security checks were skipped; this can miss shell injection, "
    57→    "unsafe deserialization, and risky SQL/subprocess patterns."
    58→)
    59→
    60→
    61→BanditRunState = Literal["ok", "missing_tool", "timeout", "error", "parse_error"]
    62→
    63→
    64→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    65→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    66→    return which(name) or name
    67→
    68→
    69→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    70→class BanditRunStatus:
    71→    """Typed execution status for a Bandit adapter invocation."""
    72→
    73→    state: BanditRunState
    74→    detail: str = ""
    75→    tool: str = "bandit"
    76→
    77→    def coverage(self) -> DetectorCoverageStatus | None:
    78→        """Convert non-success statuses into detector coverage metadata."""
    79→        if self.state == "ok":
    80→            return None
    81→
    82→        if self.state == "missing_tool":
    83→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    84→                detector="security",
    85→                status="reduced",
    86→                confidence=0.6,
    87→                summary="bandit is not installed — Python-specific security checks were skipped.",
    88→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
    89→                remediation="Install Bandit: pip install bandit",
    90→                tool=self.tool,
    91→                reason="missing_dependency",
    92→            )
    93→
    94→        if self.state == "timeout":
    95→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    96→                detector="security",
    97→                status="reduced",
    98→                confidence=0.75,
    99→                summary="bandit timed out — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   100→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   101→                remediation="Rerun scan or run `bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>` manually.",
   102→                tool=self.tool,
   103→                reason="timeout",
   104→            )
   105→
   106→        if self.state == "parse_error":
   107→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   108→                detector="security",
   109→                status="reduced",
   110→                confidence=0.75,
   111→                summary="bandit output could not be parsed — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   112→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   113→                remediation="Update/reinstall Bandit and rerun scan.",
   114→                tool=self.tool,
   115→                reason="parse_error",
   116→            )
   117→
   118→        return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   119→            detector="security",
   120→            status="reduced",
   121→            confidence=0.75,
   122→            summary="bandit failed to execute — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   123→            impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   124→            remediation="Verify Bandit is runnable and rerun scan.",
   125→            tool=self.tool,
   126→            reason="execution_error",
   127→        )
   128→
   129→
   130→@dataclass(frozen=True)
   131→class BanditScanResult:
   132→    """Bandit issues plus typed execution status."""
   133→
   134→    entries: list[dict]
   135→    files_scanned: int
   136→    status: BanditRunStatus
   137→
   138→
   139→def _to_security_entry(
   140→    result: dict,
   141→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   142→) -> dict | None:
   143→    """Convert a single bandit result dict to a security entry, or None to skip."""
   144→    filepath = str(result.get("filename", "") or "")
   145→    if not filepath:
   146→        return None
   147→
   148→    rel_path = rel(filepath)
   149→
   150→    # Apply zone filtering — only GENERATED and VENDOR are excluded for security.
   151→    if zone_map is not None:
   152→        zone = zone_map.get(rel_path)
   153→        if zone in (Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR):
   154→            return None
   155→
   156→    test_id = result.get("test_id", "")
   157→    if test_id in _CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP:
   158→        return None
   159→
   160→    raw_severity = result.get("issue_severity", "MEDIUM").upper()
   161→    raw_confidence = result.get("issue_confidence", "MEDIUM").upper()
   162→
   163→    # Suppress LOW-severity + LOW-confidence (very noisy, low signal).
   164→    if raw_severity == "LOW" and raw_confidence == "LOW":
   165→        return None
   166→
   167→    tier = _SEVERITY_TO_TIER.get(raw_severity, 3)
   168→    confidence = _SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE.get(raw_severity, "medium")
   169→
   170→    line = result.get("line_number", 0)
   171→    summary = result.get("issue_text", "")
   172→    test_name = result.get("test_name", test_id)
   173→    return {
   174→        "file": rel_path,
   175→        "name": f"security::{test_id}::{rel_path}::{line}",
   176→        "tier": tier,
   177→        "confidence": confidence,
   178→        "summary": f"[{test_id}] {summary}",
   179→        "detail": {
   180→            "kind": test_id,
   181→            "severity": raw_severity.lower(),
   182→            "line": line,
   183→            "content": result.get("code", "")[:200],
   184→            "remediation": result.get("more_info", ""),
   185→            "test_name": test_name,
   186→            "source": "bandit",
   187→        },
   188→    }
   189→
   190→
   191→def detect_with_bandit(
   192→    path: Path,
   193→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   194→    timeout: int = 120,
   195→    exclude_dirs: list[str] | None = None,
   196→) -> BanditScanResult:
   197→    """Run bandit on *path* and return issues + typed execution status.
   198→
   199→    Parameters
   200→    ----------
   201→    exclude_dirs:
   202→        Absolute directory paths to pass to bandit's ``--exclude`` flag.
   203→        When non-empty, bandit will skip these directories during its
   204→        recursive scan.
   205→    """
   206→    cmd = [
   207→        "bandit",
   208→        "-r",
   209→        "-f",
   210→        "json",
   211→        "--quiet",
   212→    ]
   213→    if exclude_dirs:
   214→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
   215→    cmd.append(str(path.resolve()))
   216→
   217→    try:
   218→        # Static bandit argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   219→        result = subprocess.run(
   220→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
   221→            capture_output=True,
   222→            text=True,
   223→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   224→            timeout=timeout,
   225→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   226→        )
   227→    except FileNotFoundError:
   228→        logger.debug("bandit: not installed — Python-specific security checks will be skipped")
   229→        return BanditScanResult(
   230→            entries=[],
   231→            files_scanned=0,
   232→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="missing_tool"),
   233→        )
   234→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   235→        logger.debug("bandit: timed out after %ds", timeout)
   236→        return BanditScanResult(
   237→            entries=[],
   238→            files_scanned=0,
   239→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="timeout", detail=f"timeout={timeout}s"),
   240→        )
   241→    except OSError as exc:
   242→        logger.debug("bandit: OSError: %s", exc)
   243→        return BanditScanResult(
   244→            entries=[],
   245→            files_scanned=0,
   246→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="error", detail=str(exc)),
   247→        )
   248→
   249→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   250→    if not stdout:
   251→        # Bandit exits 0 with no output when there's nothing to scan.
   252→        return BanditScanResult(
   253→            entries=[],
   254→            files_scanned=0,
   255→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),
   256→        )
   257→
   258→    try:
   259→        data = json.loads(stdout)
   260→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   261→        logger.debug("bandit: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   262→        return BanditScanResult(
   263→            entries=[],
   264→            files_scanned=0,
   265→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="parse_error", detail=str(exc)),
   266→        )
   267→
   268→    raw_results: list[dict] = data.get("results", [])
   269→    metrics: dict = data.get("metrics", {})
   270→
   271→    # Count scanned files from metrics (bandit reports per-file stats).
   272→    files_scanned = sum(
   273→        1
   274→        for key in metrics
   275→        if key != "_totals" and not key.endswith("_totals")
   276→    )
   277→
   278→    entries: list[dict] = []
   279→    for res in raw_results:
   280→        entry = _to_security_entry(res, zone_map)
   281→        if entry is not None:
   282→            entries.append(entry)
   283→
   284→    logger.debug("bandit: %d issues from %d files", len(entries), files_scanned)
   285→    return BanditScanResult(
   286→        entries=entries,
   287→        files_scanned=files_scanned,
   288→        status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),
   289→    )
   290→

1→"""Python smell detection via ruff (B/BLE/E/F/PGH/PLW/RUF/W-series rules).
     2→
     3→Runs ``ruff check`` with a targeted rule set and maps each ruff code to a
     4→desloppify smell ID. Produces smell entries in the same format as detect_smells()
     5→so they flow through make_smell_issues() without any plumbing changes.
     6→
     7→Falls back gracefully to [] when ruff is not installed.
     8→
     9→Rules (non-overlapping with remaining custom smells in smells.py):
    10→  B006     – mutable default argument (replaces regex in smells.py)
    11→  B007     – unused loop control variable
    12→  B023     – function definition does not bind loop variable
    13→  B026     – star-arg after keyword argument
    14→  B904     – raise inside except without ``from err``
    15→  BLE001   – blind exception catch (replaces broad_except regex)
    16→  E711     – comparison to None with == / !=
    17→  E712     – comparison to True/False with == / !=
    18→  E722     – bare except (replaces bare_except regex)
    19→  F403     – star import (replaces star_import regex; keep star_import_no_all AST)
    20→  PGH003   – blanket type: ignore (replaces type_ignore regex)
    21→  PLW0603  – global keyword (replaces global_keyword regex)
    22→  RUF012   – mutable class variable (replaces AST mutable_class_var)
    23→  W605     – invalid escape sequence (e.g. "\\d" instead of r"\\d")
    24→"""
    25→
    26→from __future__ import annotations
    27→
    28→import json
    29→import logging
    30→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    31→from collections import defaultdict
    32→from pathlib import Path
    33→from shutil import which
    34→
    35→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import collect_exclude_dirs as _collect_exclude_dirs
    36→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import get_exclusions as _get_exclusions
    37→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion as _matches_exclusion
    38→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    39→
    40→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    41→
    42→# Maps ruff code → (smell_id, label, severity)
    43→_RULE_MAP: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] = {
    44→    "B006": ("mutable_default", "Mutable default argument (list/dict/set literal)", "high"),
    45→    "B007": ("unused_loop_var", "Unused loop control variable", "medium"),
    46→    "B023": ("func_in_loop", "Function definition doesn't bind loop variable", "high"),
    47→    "B026": ("star_after_keyword", "Star-arg unpacking after keyword argument", "high"),
    48→    "B904": ("raise_without_from", "Raise inside except without `from err`", "medium"),
    49→    "BLE001": ("broad_except", "Broad except — check library exceptions before narrowing", "medium"),
    50→    "E711": ("none_comparison", "Comparison to None with == / !=  (use `is`)", "medium"),
    51→    "E712": ("bool_comparison", "Comparison to True/False with == / != (use `is`)", "low"),
    52→    "E722": ("bare_except", "Bare except clause (catches everything including SystemExit)", "high"),
    53→    "F403": ("star_import", "Star import (from X import *)", "medium"),
    54→    "PGH003": ("type_ignore", "type: ignore comment", "medium"),
    55→    "PLW0603": ("global_keyword", "Global keyword usage", "medium"),
    56→    "RUF012": ("mutable_class_var", "Class-level mutable default (shared across instances)", "high"),
    57→    "W605": ("invalid_escape", "Invalid escape sequence (use raw string or \\\\)", "medium"),
    58→}
    59→
    60→_SELECT = ",".join(_RULE_MAP)
    61→
    62→
    63→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    64→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    65→    return which(name) or name
    66→
    67→
    68→def detect_with_ruff_smells(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    69→    """Run ruff on supplemental B/E/W rules and return smell entries, or None on failure.
    70→
    71→    Each entry matches the format expected by make_smell_issues():
    72→        {
    73→            "id": smell_id,
    74→            "label": label,
    75→            "severity": "high"|"medium"|"low",
    76→            "matches": [{"file": str, "line": int}, ...],
    77→        }
    78→    """
    79→    exclude_dirs = _collect_exclude_dirs(path)
    80→    cmd = [
    81→        "ruff",
    82→        "check",
    83→        "--select",
    84→        _SELECT,
    85→        "--output-format",
    86→        "json",
    87→        "--no-fix",
    88→    ]
    89→    if exclude_dirs:
    90→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
    91→    cmd.append(str(path))
    92→
    93→    try:
    94→        # Static ruff argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    95→        result = subprocess.run(
    96→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
    97→            capture_output=True,
    98→            text=True,
    99→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   100→            timeout=60,
   101→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   102→        )
   103→    except FileNotFoundError:
   104→        logger.debug("ruff smells: ruff not found — skipping supplemental smell detection")
   105→        return None
   106→    except OSError as exc:
   107→        logger.debug("ruff smells: OS error running ruff: %s", exc)
   108→        return None
   109→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   110→        logger.debug("ruff smells: timed out")
   111→        return None
   112→
   113→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   114→    if not stdout:
   115→        return []
   116→
   117→    try:
   118→        diagnostics: list[dict] = json.loads(stdout)
   119→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   120→        logger.debug("ruff smells: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   121→        return None
   122→
   123→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   124→
   125→    # Group diagnostics by (code, file) → list of matches.
   126→    # Then convert to smell entry format.
   127→    by_code_file: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   128→    for diag in diagnostics:
   129→        code = diag.get("code", "")
   130→        if code not in _RULE_MAP:
   131→            continue
   132→        filepath = diag.get("filename", "")
   133→        if not filepath:
   134→            continue
   135→        if exclusions and any(_matches_exclusion(filepath, ex) for ex in exclusions):
   136→            continue
   137→        location = diag.get("location", {})
   138→        line = location.get("row", 0) if isinstance(location, dict) else 0
   139→        by_code_file[(code, filepath)].append({"file": filepath, "line": line})
   140→
   141→    # Aggregate by code across all files.
   142→    by_code: dict[str, dict[str, list[dict]]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
   143→    for (code, filepath), matches in by_code_file.items():
   144→        by_code[code][filepath].extend(matches)
   145→
   146→    entries: list[dict] = []
   147→    for code, files in by_code.items():
   148→        smell_id, label, severity = _RULE_MAP[code]
   149→        all_matches = [m for ms in files.values() for m in ms]
   150→        entries.append(
   151→            {
   152→                "id": smell_id,
   153→                "label": label,
   154→                "severity": severity,
   155→                "matches": all_matches,
   156→            }
   157→        )
   158→
   159→    logger.debug("ruff smells: %d supplemental smell entries", len(entries))
   160→    return entries
   161→
```

1→"""Knip adapter — dead exports detection via Knip.
     2→
     3→Runs ``npx knip --reporter json`` as a subprocess and parses its JSON output
     4→into the entry dicts expected by detect_dead_exports().
     5→
     6→Knip understands re-exports, barrel files, dynamic imports, and entry points —
     7→far more accurate than the old grep-based approach.
     8→
     9→Returns None when Knip is not installed, so the caller gets [] issues rather
    10→than a crash.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import json
    16→import logging
    17→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→from shutil import which
    20→
    21→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    27→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    28→    return which(name) or name
    29→
    30→
    31→def _run_knip(path: Path, timeout: int = 120) -> dict | None:
    32→    """Run ``npx knip --reporter json`` and return parsed JSON, or None on failure."""
    33→    try:
    34→        # Static knip argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    35→        result = subprocess.run(
    36→            [
    37→                _resolve_cli_executable("npx"),
    38→                "knip",
    39→                "--reporter",
    40→                "json",
    41→                "--no-gitignore",
    42→            ],
    43→            capture_output=True,
    44→            text=True,
    45→            cwd=str(path),
    46→            timeout=timeout,
    47→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    48→        )
    49→    except FileNotFoundError:
    50→        logger.debug("knip: npx not found")
    51→        return None
    52→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    53→        logger.debug("knip: timed out after %ds", timeout)
    54→        return None
    55→    except OSError as exc:
    56→        logger.debug("knip: OSError: %s", exc)
    57→        return None
    58→
    59→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
    60→    if not stdout:
    61→        return None
    62→
    63→    try:
    64→        return json.loads(stdout)
    65→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    66→        logger.debug("knip: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
    67→        return None
    68→
    69→
    70→def _normalize_path(raw: str, scan_path: Path) -> str:
    71→    """Return a path relative to PROJECT_ROOT, scoped to scan_path."""
    72→    p = Path(raw)
    73→    if not p.is_absolute():
    74→        p = (scan_path / p).resolve()
    75→    try:
    76→        p.relative_to(scan_path.resolve())
    77→    except ValueError:
    78→        return ""
    79→    return rel(str(p))
    80→
    81→
    82→def detect_with_knip(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    83→    """Run Knip and return export entries, or None if Knip is unavailable.
    84→
    85→    Each entry: {"file": str, "name": str, "line": int, "kind": "export"|"type"}
    86→    """
    87→    data = _run_knip(path)
    88→    if data is None:
    89→        return None
    90→
    91→    entries: list[dict] = []
    92→
    93→    for issue in data.get("issues", []):
    94→        raw_file = issue.get("file", "")
    95→        norm = _normalize_path(raw_file, path)
    96→        if not norm:
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        for export in issue.get("exports", []):
   100→            name = export.get("name", "")
   101→            if not name:
   102→                continue
   103→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
   104→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
   105→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   106→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   107→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   108→                line = raw_pos
   109→            else:
   110→                line = 0
   111→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "export"})
   112→
   113→        for export in issue.get("types", []):
   114→            name = export.get("name", "")
   115→            if not name:
   116→                continue
   117→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
   118→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
   119→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   120→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   121→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   122→                line = raw_pos
   123→            else:
   124→                line = 0
   125→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "type"})
   126→
   127→    logger.debug("knip: %d dead exports", len(entries))
   128→    return entries
   129→
```
