1→"""Bandit adapter — Python security detection via the bandit static analyser.
     2→
     3→Runs ``bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>`` as a subprocess and converts its JSON
     4→output into the security entry dicts expected by ``phase_security``.
     5→
     6→Bandit covers AST-level security checks (shell injection, unsafe deserialization,
     7→SQL injection, etc.) more reliably than custom regex/AST patterns. When bandit is
     8→installed, it is used as the lang-specific security detector; otherwise
     9→Python-specific security checks will be skipped.
    10→
    11→Bandit severity → desloppify tier/confidence mapping:
    12→  HIGH   → tier=4, confidence="high"
    13→  MEDIUM → tier=3, confidence="medium"
    14→  LOW    → tier=3, confidence="low"
    15→
    16→The ``check_id`` in the entry detail is the bandit test ID (e.g., "B602") so
    17→issues are stable across reruns and can be wontfix-tracked by ID.
    18→"""
    19→
    20→from __future__ import annotations
    21→
    22→import json
    23→import logging
    24→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    25→from dataclasses import dataclass
    26→from pathlib import Path
    27→from shutil import which
    28→from typing import Literal
    29→
    30→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    31→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    32→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap, Zone
    33→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import DetectorCoverageStatus
    34→
    35→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    36→
    37→_SEVERITY_TO_TIER = {"HIGH": 4, "MEDIUM": 3, "LOW": 3}
    38→_SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE = {"HIGH": "high", "MEDIUM": "medium", "LOW": "low"}
    39→
    40→# Bandit test IDs that overlap with the cross-language security detector
    41→# (secret names, hardcoded passwords). Skip these to avoid duplicate issues.
    42→_CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP = frozenset(
    43→    {
    44→        "B105",  # hardcoded_password_string
    45→        "B106",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    46→        "B107",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    47→        "B501",  # request_with_no_cert_validation  (covered by weak_crypto_tls)
    48→        "B502",  # ssl_with_bad_version
    49→        "B503",  # ssl_with_bad_defaults
    50→        "B504",  # ssl_with_no_version
    51→        "B505",  # weak_cryptographic_key
    52→    }
    53→)
    54→
    55→_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT = (
    56→    "Python-specific security checks were skipped; this can miss shell injection, "
    57→    "unsafe deserialization, and risky SQL/subprocess patterns."
    58→)
    59→
    60→
    61→BanditRunState = Literal["ok", "missing_tool", "timeout", "error", "parse_error"]
    62→
    63→
    64→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    65→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    66→    return which(name) or name
    67→
    68→
    69→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    70→class BanditRunStatus:
    71→    """Typed execution status for a Bandit adapter invocation."""
    72→
    73→    state: BanditRunState
    74→    detail: str = ""
    75→    tool: str = "bandit"
    76→
    77→    def coverage(self) -> DetectorCoverageStatus | None:
    78→        """Convert non-success statuses into detector coverage metadata."""
    79→        if self.state == "ok":
    80→            return None
    81→
    82→        if self.state == "missing_tool":
    83→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    84→                detector="security",
    85→                status="reduced",
    86→                confidence=0.6,
    87→                summary="bandit is not installed — Python-specific security checks were skipped.",
    88→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
    89→                remediation="Install Bandit: pip install bandit",
    90→                tool=self.tool,
    91→                reason="missing_dependency",
    92→            )
    93→
    94→        if self.state == "timeout":
    95→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    96→                detector="security",
    97→                status="reduced",
    98→                confidence=0.75,
    99→                summary="bandit timed out — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   100→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   101→                remediation="Rerun scan or run `bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>` manually.",
   102→                tool=self.tool,
   103→                reason="timeout",
   104→            )
   105→
   106→        if self.state == "parse_error":
   107→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   108→                detector="security",
   109→                status="reduced",
   110→                confidence=0.75,
   111→                summary="bandit output could not be parsed — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   112→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   113→                remediation="Update/reinstall Bandit and rerun scan.",
   114→                tool=self.tool,
   115→                reason="parse_error",
   116→            )
   117→
   118→        return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   119→            detector="security",
   120→            status="reduced",
   121→            confidence=0.75,
   122→            summary="bandit failed to execute — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   123→            impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   124→            remediation="Verify Bandit is runnable and rerun scan.",
   125→            tool=self.tool,
   126→            reason="execution_error",
   127→        )
   128→
   129→
   130→@dataclass(frozen=True)
   131→class BanditScanResult:
   132→    """Bandit issues plus typed execution status."""
   133→
   134→    entries: list[dict]
   135→    files_scanned: int
   136→    status: BanditRunStatus
   137→
   138→
   139→def _to_security_entry(
   140→    result: dict,
   141→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   142→) -> dict | None:
   143→    """Convert a single bandit result dict to a security entry, or None to skip."""
   144→    filepath = str(result.get("filename", "") or "")
   145→    if not filepath:
   146→        return None
   147→
   148→    rel_path = rel(filepath)
   149→
   150→    # Apply zone filtering — only GENERATED and VENDOR are excluded for security.
   151→    if zone_map is not None:
   152→        zone = zone_map.get(rel_path)
   153→        if zone in (Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR):
   154→            return None
   155→
   156→    test_id = result.get("test_id", "")
   157→    if test_id in _CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP:
   158→        return None
   159→
   160→    raw_severity = result.get("issue_severity", "MEDIUM").upper()
   161→    raw_confidence = result.get("issue_confidence", "MEDIUM").upper()
   162→
   163→    # Suppress LOW-severity + LOW-confidence (very noisy, low signal).
   164→    if raw_severity == "LOW" and raw_confidence == "LOW":
   165→        return None
   166→
   167→    tier = _SEVERITY_TO_TIER.get(raw_severity, 3)
   168→    confidence = _SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE.get(raw_severity, "medium")
   169→
   170→    line = result.get("line_number", 0)
   171→    summary = result.get("issue_text", "")
   172→    test_name = result.get("test_name", test_id)
   173→    return {
   174→        "file": rel_path,
   175→        "name": f"security::{test_id}::{rel_path}::{line}",
   176→        "tier": tier,
   177→        "confidence": confidence,
   178→        "summary": f"[{test_id}] {summary}",
   179→        "detail": {
   180→            "kind": test_id,
   181→            "severity": raw_severity.lower(),
   182→            "line": line,
   183→            "content": result.get("code", "")[:200],
   184→            "remediation": result.get("more_info", ""),
   185→            "test_name": test_name,
   186→            "source": "bandit",
   187→        },
   188→    }
   189→
   190→
   191→def detect_with_bandit(
   192→    path: Path,
   193→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   194→    timeout: int = 120,
   195→    exclude_dirs: list[str] | None = None,
   196→) -> BanditScanResult:
   197→    """Run bandit on *path* and return issues + typed execution status.
   198→
   199→    Parameters
   200→    ----------
   201→    exclude_dirs:
   202→        Absolute directory paths to pass to bandit's ``--exclude`` flag.
   203→        When non-empty, bandit will skip these directories during its
   204→        recursive scan.
   205→    """
   206→    cmd = [
   207→        "bandit",
   208→        "-r",
   209→        "-f",
   210→        "json",
   211→        "--quiet",
   212→    ]
   213→    if exclude_dirs:
   214→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
   215→    cmd.append(str(path.resolve()))
   216→
   217→    try:
   218→        # Static bandit argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   219→        result = subprocess.run(
   220→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
   221→            capture_output=True,
   222→            text=True,
   223→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   224→            timeout=timeout,
   225→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   226→        )
   227→    except FileNotFoundError:
   228→        logger.debug("bandit: not installed — Python-specific security checks will be skipped")
   229→        return BanditScanResult(
   230→            entries=[],
   231→            files_scanned=0,
   232→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="missing_tool"),
   233→        )
   234→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   235→        logger.debug("bandit: timed out after %ds", timeout)
   236→        return BanditScanResult(
   237→            entries=[],
   238→            files_scanned=0,
   239→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="timeout", detail=f"timeout={timeout}s"),
   240→        )
   241→    except OSError as exc:
   242→        logger.debug("bandit: OSError: %s", exc)
   243→        return BanditScanResult(
   244→            entries=[],
   245→            files_scanned=0,
   246→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="error", detail=str(exc)),
   247→        )
   248→
   249→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   250→    if not stdout:
   251→        # Bandit exits 0 with no output when there's nothing to scan.
   252→        return BanditScanResult(
   253→            entries=[],
   254→            files_scanned=0,
   255→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),
   256→        )
   257→
   258→    try:
   259→        data = json.loads(stdout)

   261→        logger.debug("bandit: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   262→        return BanditScanResult(
   263→            entries=[],
   264→            files_scanned=0,
   265→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="parse_error", detail=str(exc)),
   266→        )
   267→
   268→    raw_results: list[dict] = data.get("results", [])
   269→    metrics: dict = data.get("metrics", {})
   270→
   271→    # Count scanned files from metrics (bandit reports per-file stats).
   272→    files_scanned = sum(
   273→        1
   274→        for key in metrics
   275→        if key != "_totals" and not key.endswith("_totals")
   276→    )
   277→
   278→    entries: list[dict] = []
   279→    for res in raw_results:
   280→        entry = _to_security_entry(res, zone_map)
   281→        if entry is not None:
   282→            entries.append(entry)
   283→
   284→    logger.debug("bandit: %d issues from %d files", len(entries), files_scanned)
   285→    return BanditScanResult(
   286→        entries=entries,
   287→        files_scanned=files_scanned,
   288→        status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),

1→"""Python smell detection via ruff (B/BLE/E/F/PGH/PLW/RUF/W-series rules).
     2→
     3→Runs ``ruff check`` with a targeted rule set and maps each ruff code to a
     4→desloppify smell ID. Produces smell entries in the same format as detect_smells()
     5→so they flow through make_smell_issues() without any plumbing changes.
     6→
     7→Falls back gracefully to [] when ruff is not installed.
     8→
     9→Rules (non-overlapping with remaining custom smells in smells.py):
    10→  B006     – mutable default argument (replaces regex in smells.py)
    11→  B007     – unused loop control variable
    12→  B023     – function definition does not bind loop variable
    13→  B026     – star-arg after keyword argument
    14→  B904     – raise inside except without ``from err``
    15→  BLE001   – blind exception catch (replaces broad_except regex)
    16→  E711     – comparison to None with == / !=
    17→  E712     – comparison to True/False with == / !=
    18→  E722     – bare except (replaces bare_except regex)
    19→  F403     – star import (replaces star_import regex; keep star_import_no_all AST)
    20→  PGH003   – blanket type: ignore (replaces type_ignore regex)
    21→  PLW0603  – global keyword (replaces global_keyword regex)
    22→  RUF012   – mutable class variable (replaces AST mutable_class_var)
    23→  W605     – invalid escape sequence (e.g. "\\d" instead of r"\\d")
    24→"""
    25→
    26→from __future__ import annotations
    27→
    28→import json
    29→import logging
    30→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    31→from collections import defaultdict
    32→from pathlib import Path
    33→from shutil import which
    34→
    35→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import collect_exclude_dirs as _collect_exclude_dirs
    36→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import get_exclusions as _get_exclusions
    37→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion as _matches_exclusion
    38→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    39→
    40→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    41→
    42→# Maps ruff code → (smell_id, label, severity)
    43→_RULE_MAP: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] = {
    44→    "B006": ("mutable_default", "Mutable default argument (list/dict/set literal)", "high"),
    45→    "B007": ("unused_loop_var", "Unused loop control variable", "medium"),
    46→    "B023": ("func_in_loop", "Function definition doesn't bind loop variable", "high"),
    47→    "B026": ("star_after_keyword", "Star-arg unpacking after keyword argument", "high"),
    48→    "B904": ("raise_without_from", "Raise inside except without `from err`", "medium"),
    49→    "BLE001": ("broad_except", "Broad except — check library exceptions before narrowing", "medium"),
    50→    "E711": ("none_comparison", "Comparison to None with == / !=  (use `is`)", "medium"),
    51→    "E712": ("bool_comparison", "Comparison to True/False with == / != (use `is`)", "low"),
    52→    "E722": ("bare_except", "Bare except clause (catches everything including SystemExit)", "high"),
    53→    "F403": ("star_import", "Star import (from X import *)", "medium"),
    54→    "PGH003": ("type_ignore", "type: ignore comment", "medium"),
    55→    "PLW0603": ("global_keyword", "Global keyword usage", "medium"),
    56→    "RUF012": ("mutable_class_var", "Class-level mutable default (shared across instances)", "high"),
    57→    "W605": ("invalid_escape", "Invalid escape sequence (use raw string or \\\\)", "medium"),
    58→}
    59→
    60→_SELECT = ",".join(_RULE_MAP)
    61→
    62→
    63→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    64→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    65→    return which(name) or name
    66→
    67→
    68→def detect_with_ruff_smells(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    69→    """Run ruff on supplemental B/E/W rules and return smell entries, or None on failure.
    70→
    71→    Each entry matches the format expected by make_smell_issues():
    72→        {
    73→            "id": smell_id,
    74→            "label": label,
    75→            "severity": "high"|"medium"|"low",
    76→            "matches": [{"file": str, "line": int}, ...],
    77→        }
    78→    """
    79→    exclude_dirs = _collect_exclude_dirs(path)
    80→    cmd = [
    81→        "ruff",
    82→        "check",
    83→        "--select",
    84→        _SELECT,
    85→        "--output-format",
    86→        "json",
    87→        "--no-fix",
    88→    ]
    89→    if exclude_dirs:
    90→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
    91→    cmd.append(str(path))
    92→
    93→    try:
    94→        # Static ruff argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    95→        result = subprocess.run(
    96→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
    97→            capture_output=True,
    98→            text=True,
    99→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   100→            timeout=60,
   101→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   102→        )
   103→    except FileNotFoundError:
   104→        logger.debug("ruff smells: ruff not found — skipping supplemental smell detection")
   105→        return None
   106→    except OSError as exc:
   107→        logger.debug("ruff smells: OS error running ruff: %s", exc)
   108→        return None
   109→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   110→        logger.debug("ruff smells: timed out")
   111→        return None
   112→
   113→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   114→    if not stdout:
   115→        return []
   116→
   117→    try:
   118→        diagnostics: list[dict] = json.loads(stdout)
   119→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   120→        logger.debug("ruff smells: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   121→        return None
   122→
   123→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   124→
   125→    # Group diagnostics by (code, file) → list of matches.
   126→    # Then convert to smell entry format.
   127→    by_code_file: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   128→    for diag in diagnostics:
   129→        code = diag.get("code", "")
   130→        if code not in _RULE_MAP:
   131→            continue
   132→        filepath = diag.get("filename", "")
   133→        if not filepath:
   134→            continue
   135→        if exclusions and any(_matches_exclusion(filepath, ex) for ex in exclusions):
   136→            continue
   137→        location = diag.get("location", {})
   138→        line = location.get("row", 0) if isinstance(location, dict) else 0
   139→        by_code_file[(code, filepath)].append({"file": filepath, "line": line})
   140→
   141→    # Aggregate by code across all files.
   142→    by_code: dict[str, dict[str, list[dict]]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
   143→    for (code, filepath), matches in by_code_file.items():
   144→        by_code[code][filepath].extend(matches)
   145→
   146→    entries: list[dict] = []
   147→    for code, files in by_code.items():
   148→        smell_id, label, severity = _RULE_MAP[code]
   149→        all_matches = [m for ms in files.values() for m in ms]
   150→        entries.append(
   151→            {
   152→                "id": smell_id,
   153→                "label": label,
   154→                "severity": severity,
   155→                "matches": all_matches,
   156→            }
   157→        )
   158→
   159→    logger.debug("ruff smells: %d supplemental smell entries", len(entries))
   160→    return entries
   161→
```

1→"""Knip adapter — dead exports detection via Knip.
     2→
     3→Runs ``npx knip --reporter json`` as a subprocess and parses its JSON output
     4→into the entry dicts expected by detect_dead_exports().
     5→
     6→Knip understands re-exports, barrel files, dynamic imports, and entry points —
     7→far more accurate than the old grep-based approach.
     8→
     9→Returns None when Knip is not installed, so the caller gets [] issues rather
    10→than a crash.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import json
    16→import logging
    17→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→from shutil import which
    20→
    21→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    27→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    28→    return which(name) or name
    29→
    30→
    31→def _run_knip(path: Path, timeout: int = 120) -> dict | None:
    32→    """Run ``npx knip --reporter json`` and return parsed JSON, or None on failure."""
    33→    try:
    34→        # Static knip argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    35→        result = subprocess.run(
    36→            [
    37→                _resolve_cli_executable("npx"),
    38→                "knip",
    39→                "--reporter",
    40→                "json",
    41→                "--no-gitignore",
    42→            ],
    43→            capture_output=True,
    44→            text=True,
    45→            cwd=str(path),
    46→            timeout=timeout,
    47→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    48→        )
    49→    except FileNotFoundError:
    50→        logger.debug("knip: npx not found")
    51→        return None
    52→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    53→        logger.debug("knip: timed out after %ds", timeout)
    54→        return None
    55→    except OSError as exc:
    56→        logger.debug("knip: OSError: %s", exc)
    57→        return None
    58→
    59→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
    60→    if not stdout:
    61→        return None
    62→
    63→    try:
    64→        return json.loads(stdout)
    65→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    66→        logger.debug("knip: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
    67→        return None
    68→
    69→
    70→def _normalize_path(raw: str, scan_path: Path) -> str:
    71→    """Return a path relative to PROJECT_ROOT, scoped to scan_path."""
    72→    p = Path(raw)
    73→    if not p.is_absolute():
    74→        p = (scan_path / p).resolve()
    75→    try:
    76→        p.relative_to(scan_path.resolve())
    77→    except ValueError:
    78→        return ""
    79→    return rel(str(p))
    80→
    81→
    82→def detect_with_knip(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    83→    """Run Knip and return export entries, or None if Knip is unavailable.
    84→
    85→    Each entry: {"file": str, "name": str, "line": int, "kind": "export"|"type"}
    86→    """
    87→    data = _run_knip(path)
    88→    if data is None:
    89→        return None
    90→
    91→    entries: list[dict] = []
    92→
    93→    for issue in data.get("issues", []):
    94→        raw_file = issue.get("file", "")
    95→        norm = _normalize_path(raw_file, path)
    96→        if not norm:
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        for export in issue.get("exports", []):
   100→            name = export.get("name", "")
   101→            if not name:
   102→                continue
   103→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
   104→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
   105→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   106→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   107→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   108→                line = raw_pos
   109→            else:
   110→                line = 0
   111→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "export"})
   112→
   113→        for export in issue.get("types", []):
   114→            name = export.get("name", "")
   115→            if not name:
   116→                continue
   117→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
   118→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
   119→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   120→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   121→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   122→                line = raw_pos
   123→            else:
   124→                line = 0
   125→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "type"})
   126→
   127→    logger.debug("knip: %d dead exports", len(entries))
   128→    return entries
   129→
```

1→"""Unused declarations detection via tsc TS6133/TS6192.
     2→
     3→Includes a Deno/edge-functions fallback where `tsc` cannot model URL-based imports.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→import argparse
     9→import json
    10→import logging
    11→import re
    12→import subprocess  # nosec B404
    13→from collections import defaultdict
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→from shutil import which
    16→
    17→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel, resolve_path, safe_write_text
    18→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    19→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_ts_and_tsx_files
    20→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize, print_table
    21→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors.unused_fallback import (
    22→    _contains_deno_markers,
    23→    _extract_import_names,
    24→    _has_deno_import_syntax,
    25→    _identifier_occurrences,
    26→    detect_unused_fallback,
    27→    should_use_deno_fallback,
    28→)
    29→
    30→TS6133_RE = re.compile(
    31→    r"^(.+)\((\d+),(\d+)\): error TS6133: '(\S+)' is declared but its value is never read\."
    32→)
    33→TS6192_RE = re.compile(
    34→    r"^(.+)\((\d+),(\d+)\): error TS6192: All imports in import declaration are unused\."
    35→)
    36→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    37→
    38→# Compatibility aliases for external callers/tests that imported private names.
    39→_detect_unused_fallback = detect_unused_fallback
    40→_should_use_deno_fallback = should_use_deno_fallback
    41→
    42→
    43→def _resolve_cli_executable(name: str) -> str:
    44→    """Return an absolute CLI path when available."""
    45→    return which(name) or name
    46→
    47→
    48→def detect_unused(path: Path, category: str = "all") -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    49→    ts_files = find_ts_and_tsx_files(path)
    50→    total_files = len(ts_files)
    51→    if _should_use_deno_fallback(path, ts_files):
    52→        return _detect_unused_fallback(path, category)
    53→
    54→    tmp_tsconfig = {
    55→        "extends": "./tsconfig.app.json",
    56→        "compilerOptions": {
    57→            "noUnusedLocals": True,
    58→            "noUnusedParameters": True,
    59→        },
    60→    }
    61→    tmp_path = get_project_root() / "tsconfig.desloppify.json"
    62→    try:
    63→        safe_write_text(tmp_path, json.dumps(tmp_tsconfig, indent=2))
    64→        try:
    65→            # Static tsc argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    66→            result = subprocess.run(
    67→                [
    68→                    _resolve_cli_executable("npx"),
    69→                    "tsc",
    70→                    "--project",
    71→                    str(tmp_path),
    72→                    "--noEmit",
    73→                ],
    74→                capture_output=True,
    75→                text=True,
    76→                cwd=get_project_root(),
    77→                timeout=120,
    78→                shell=False,  # nosec B603
    79→            )
    80→        except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as exc:
    81→            logger.debug("Falling back to source-based unused detection: %s", exc)
    82→            return _detect_unused_fallback(path, category)
    83→    finally:
    84→        tmp_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    85→
    86→    entries = []
    87→    for line in result.stdout.splitlines() + result.stderr.splitlines():
    88→        m = TS6133_RE.match(line)
    89→        m2 = TS6192_RE.match(line) if not m else None
    90→        if not m and not m2:
    91→            continue
    92→        if m:
    93→            filepath, lineno, col, name = (
    94→                m.group(1),
    95→                int(m.group(2)),
    96→                int(m.group(3)),
    97→                m.group(4),
    98→            )
    99→            if name.startswith("_"):
   100→                continue
   101→        else:
   102→            filepath, lineno, col = m2.group(1), int(m2.group(2)), int(m2.group(3))
   103→            name = "(entire import)"
   104→
   105→        try:
   106→            full = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
   107→            if not str(full).startswith(str(path.resolve())):
   108→                continue
   109→        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
   110→            logger.debug("Skipping path scope check for %s: %s", filepath, exc)
   111→            continue
   112→
   113→        cat = _categorize_unused(filepath, lineno)
   114→        if category != "all" and cat != category:
   115→            continue
   116→        entries.append(
   117→            {
   118→                "file": filepath,
   119→                "line": lineno,
   120→                "col": col,
   121→                "name": name,
   122→                "category": cat,
   123→            }
   124→        )
   125→    return entries, total_files
   126→
   127→
   128→def _categorize_unused(filepath: str, lineno: int) -> str:
   129→    try:
   130→        p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
   131→        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
   132→        if lineno <= len(lines):
   133→            src_line = lines[lineno - 1].strip()
   134→            if src_line.startswith("import ") or "from '" in src_line or 'from "' in src_line:
   135→                return "imports"
   136→            if src_line.startswith(
   137→                (
   138→                    "const ",
   139→                    "let ",
   140→                    "var ",
   141→                    "export ",
   142→                    "function ",
   143→                    "class ",
   144→                    "type ",
   145→                    "interface ",
   146→                )
   147→            ):
   148→                return "vars"
   149→            for back in range(1, 10):
   150→                idx = lineno - 1 - back
   151→                if idx < 0:
   152→                    break
   153→                prev = lines[idx].strip()
   154→                if prev.startswith("import "):
   155→                    return "imports"
   156→                if not prev or (
   157→                    not prev.startswith("{") and not prev.startswith(",") and "," not in prev
   158→                ):
   159→                    break
   160→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   161→        logger.debug("Unable to read %s for unused categorization: %s", filepath, exc)
   162→        return "imports"
   163→    return "imports"
   164→
   165→
   166→def cmd_unused(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   167→    path = Path(args.path)
   168→    if _should_use_deno_fallback(path, find_ts_and_tsx_files(path)):
   169→        print(
   170→            colorize(
   171→                "Deno/edge TypeScript context detected — using source-based unused scan",
   172→                "dim",
   173→            ),
   174→            file=sys.stderr,
   175→        )
   176→    else:
   177→        print(colorize("Running tsc... (this may take a moment)", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   178→
   179→    entries, _ = detect_unused(path, args.category)
   180→    if args.json:
   181→        print(json.dumps({"count": len(entries), "entries": entries}, indent=2))
   182→        return
   183→
   184→    if not entries:
   185→        print(colorize("No unused declarations found.", "green"))
   186→        return
   187→
   188→    by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
   189→    for entry in entries:
   190→        by_file[entry["file"]].append(entry)
   191→
   192→    by_cat: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   193→    for entry in entries:
   194→        by_cat[entry["category"]] += 1
   195→
   196→    print(
   197→        colorize(
   198→            f"\nUnused declarations: {len(entries)} across {len(by_file)} files\n",
   199→            "bold",
   200→        )
   201→    )
   202→
   203→    print(colorize("By category:", "cyan"))
   204→    for cat, count in sorted(by_cat.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1]):
   205→        print(f"  {cat}: {count}")
   206→    print()
   207→
   208→    print(colorize("Top files:", "cyan"))
   209→    sorted_files = sorted(by_file.items(), key=lambda item: -len(item[1]))
   210→    rows = []
   211→    for filepath, file_entries in sorted_files[: args.top]:
   212→        names = ", ".join(entry["name"] for entry in file_entries[:5])
   213→        if len(file_entries) > 5:
   214→            names += f", ... (+{len(file_entries) - 5})"
   215→        rows.append([rel(filepath), str(len(file_entries)), names])
   216→    print_table(["File", "Count", "Names"], rows, [55, 6, 50])
   217→
   218→
   219→__all__ = [
   220→    "TS6133_RE",
   221→    "TS6192_RE",
   222→    "_categorize_unused",
   223→    "_contains_deno_markers",
   224→    "_detect_unused_fallback",
   225→    "_extract_import_names",
   226→    "_has_deno_import_syntax",
   227→    "_identifier_occurrences",
   228→    "_should_use_deno_fallback",
   229→    "cmd_unused",
   230→    "detect_unused",
   231→]
   232→
```

1→"""Read-only git observation with graceful degradation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import subprocess
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass
     8→
     9→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    10→
    11→_GIT_TIMEOUT = 5
    12→
    13→
    14→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    15→class GitContext:
    16→    available: bool
    17→    branch: str | None = None
    18→    head_sha: str | None = None
    19→    has_uncommitted: bool = False
    20→    root: str | None = None
    21→
    22→
    23→def detect_git_context() -> GitContext:
    24→    """Detect current git context (branch, HEAD, uncommitted changes).
    25→
    26→    Returns ``available=False`` when git is missing or not in a repo.
    27→    """
    28→    git_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("git")
    29→    try:
    30→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    31→        head = subprocess.run(
    32→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "HEAD"],
    33→            capture_output=True,
    34→            text=True,
    35→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    36→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    37→        )
    38→        if head.returncode != 0:
    39→            return GitContext(available=False)
    40→
    41→        sha = head.stdout.strip()[:12]
    42→
    43→        branch_result = subprocess.run(
    44→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "--abbrev-ref", "HEAD"],
    45→            capture_output=True,
    46→            text=True,
    47→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    48→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    49→        )
    50→        branch = branch_result.stdout.strip() if branch_result.returncode == 0 else None
    51→
    52→        root_result = subprocess.run(
    53→            [git_cli, "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
    54→            capture_output=True,
    55→            text=True,
    56→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    57→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    58→        )
    59→        root = root_result.stdout.strip() if root_result.returncode == 0 else None
    60→
    61→        status_result = subprocess.run(
    62→            [git_cli, "status", "--porcelain"],
    63→            capture_output=True,
    64→            text=True,
    65→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT,
    66→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    67→        )
    68→        has_uncommitted = bool(status_result.stdout.strip()) if status_result.returncode == 0 else False
    69→
    70→        return GitContext(
    71→            available=True,
    72→            branch=branch,
    73→            head_sha=sha,
    74→            has_uncommitted=has_uncommitted,
    75→            root=root,
    76→        )
    77→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError) as exc:
    78→        logger.debug("git context unavailable: %s", exc)
    79→        return GitContext(available=False)
    80→
    81→
    82→def update_pr_body(pr_number: int, body: str) -> bool:
    83→    """Update PR description via ``gh pr edit``.  Returns True on success."""
    84→    gh_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("gh")
    85→    try:
    86→        # Static gh argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    87→        result = subprocess.run(
    88→            [gh_cli, "pr", "edit", str(pr_number), "--body", body],
    89→            capture_output=True,
    90→            text=True,
    91→            timeout=_GIT_TIMEOUT * 3,
    92→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    93→        )
    94→        if result.returncode != 0:
    95→            logger.warning("gh pr edit failed: %s", result.stderr.strip())
    96→            return False
    97→        return True
    98→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError) as exc:
    99→        logger.warning("gh pr edit unavailable: %s", exc)
   100→        return False
   101→
   102→
   103→__all__ = ["GitContext", "detect_git_context", "update_pr_body"]
   104→

1→"""Metadata helpers for scorecard rendering."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import re
     7→import subprocess
     8→from collections.abc import Callable
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    12→
    13→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    14→
    15→
    16→def resolve_project_name(project_root: Path) -> str:
    17→    """Resolve owner/repo display name from GitHub CLI, git remote, or folder."""
    18→    gh_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("gh")
    19→    try:
    20→        # Static gh argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    21→        name = subprocess.check_output(
    22→            [
    23→                gh_cli,
    24→                "repo",
    25→                "view",
    26→                "--json",
    27→                "nameWithOwner",
    28→                "-q",
    29→                ".nameWithOwner",
    30→            ],
    31→            cwd=str(project_root),
    32→            stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL,
    33→            text=True,
    34→            timeout=5,
    35→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    36→        ).strip()
    37→        if "/" in name:
    38→            return name
    39→    except (
    40→        subprocess.CalledProcessError,
    41→        FileNotFoundError,
    42→        subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
    43→    ) as exc:
    44→        logger.debug("gh repo view failed, falling back to git remote: %s", exc)
    45→
    46→    try:
    47→        git_cli = _resolve_cli_executable("git")
    48→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    49→        url = subprocess.check_output(
    50→            [git_cli, "config", "--get", "remote.origin.url"],
    51→            cwd=str(project_root),
    52→            stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL,
    53→            text=True,
    54→            timeout=5,
    55→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    56→        ).strip()
    57→        if url.startswith("git@") and ":" in url:
    58→            path = url.split(":")[-1]
    59→        else:
    60→            path = "/".join(url.split("/")[-2:])
    61→        return path.removesuffix(".git")
    62→    except (
    63→        subprocess.CalledProcessError,
    64→        FileNotFoundError,
    65→        IndexError,
    66→        subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
    67→    ) as exc:
    68→        logger.debug("git remote lookup failed, falling back to folder name: %s", exc)
    69→        return project_root.name
    70→
    71→
    72→def resolve_package_version(
    73→    project_root: Path,
    74→    *,
    75→    version_getter: Callable[[str], str],
    76→    package_not_found_error: type[Exception],
    77→) -> str:
    78→    """Resolve package version from installed metadata or local pyproject."""
    79→    try:
    80→        return version_getter("desloppify")
    81→    except package_not_found_error as exc:
    82→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read package metadata version", exc)
    83→
    84→    pyproject_path = project_root / "pyproject.toml"
    85→    try:
    86→        text = pyproject_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    87→        match = re.search(r'^\s*version\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"\s*$', text, re.MULTILINE)
    88→        if match:
    89→            return match.group(1)
    90→    except OSError as exc:
    91→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read pyproject.toml for package version", exc)
    92→
    93→    return "unknown"
    94→

1→"""Retro/checklist and git-safety helpers for autofix apply flow."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import subprocess
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    11→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    12→from desloppify.languages.framework import FixResult
    13→
    14→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    15→    from desloppify.languages.framework import LangRun
    16→
    17→
    18→def _resolve_fixer_results(
    19→    state: dict, results: list[dict], detector: str, fixer_name: str
    20→) -> list[str]:
    21→    resolved_ids = []
    22→    for result in results:
    23→        result_file = rel(result["file"])
    24→        for symbol in result["removed"]:
    25→            issue_id = f"{detector}::{result_file}::{symbol}"
    26→            if issue_id in state["issues"] and state["issues"][issue_id]["status"] == "open":
    27→                state["issues"][issue_id]["status"] = "fixed"
    28→                state["issues"][issue_id]["note"] = (
    29→                    f"auto-fixed by desloppify autofix {fixer_name}"
    30→                )
    31→                resolved_ids.append(issue_id)
    32→    return resolved_ids
    33→
    34→
    35→def _warn_uncommitted_changes() -> None:
    36→    git_executable = which("git") or "git"
    37→    try:
    38→        # Static git argv only; no shell expansion or user-provided executable path.
    39→        result = subprocess.run(
    40→            [git_executable, "status", "--porcelain"],
    41→            capture_output=True,
    42→            text=True,
    43→            timeout=5,
    44→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    45→        )
    46→        if result.stdout.strip():
    47→            print(colorize("\n  ⚠ You have uncommitted changes. Consider running:", "yellow"))
    48→            print(
    49→                colorize(
    50→                    "    git add -A && git commit -m 'pre-fix checkpoint' && git push",
    51→                    "yellow",
    52→                )
    53→            )
    54→            print(
    55→                colorize(
    56→                    "    This ensures you can revert if the fixer produces unexpected results.\n",
    57→                    "dim",
    58→                )
    59→            )
    60→    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
    61→        return
    62→
    63→
    64→def _cascade_unused_import_cleanup(
    65→    path: Path,
    66→    state: dict,
    67→    _prev_score: float,
    68→    dry_run: bool,
    69→    *,
    70→    lang: LangRun | None = None,
    71→) -> None:
    72→    if not lang or "unused-imports" not in getattr(lang, "fixers", {}):
    73→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no unused-imports fixer for this language", "dim"))
    74→        return
    75→
    76→    fixer = lang.fixers["unused-imports"]
    77→    print(colorize("\n  Running cascading import cleanup...", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    78→    entries = fixer.detect(path)
    79→    if not entries:
    80→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
    81→        return
    82→
    83→    raw_results = fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
    84→    results = raw_results.entries if isinstance(raw_results, FixResult) else raw_results
    85→
    86→    if not results:
    87→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
    88→        return
    89→
    90→    removed_count = sum(len(result["removed"]) for result in results)
    91→    removed_lines = sum(result["lines_removed"] for result in results)
    92→    print(
    93→        colorize(
    94→            f"  Cascade: removed {removed_count} now-orphaned imports "
    95→            f"from {len(results)} files ({removed_lines} lines)",
    96→            "green",
    97→        )
    98→    )
    99→    resolved = _resolve_fixer_results(
   100→        state, results, fixer.detector, "cascade-unused-imports"
   101→    )
   102→    if resolved:
   103→        print(f"  Cascade: auto-resolved {len(resolved)} import issues")
   104→
   105→
   106→_SKIP_REASON_LABELS = {
   107→    "rest_element": "has ...rest (removing changes rest contents)",
   108→    "array_destructuring": "array destructuring (positional — can't remove)",
   109→    "function_param": "function/callback parameter (use `fix unused-params` to prefix with _)",
   110→    "standalone_var_with_call": "standalone variable with function call (may have side effects)",
   111→    "no_destr_context": "destructuring member without context",
   112→    "out_of_range": "line out of range (stale data?)",
   113→    "other": "other patterns (needs manual review)",
   114→}
   115→
   116→
   117→def _print_fix_retro(
   118→    fixer_name: str,
   119→    detected: int,
   120→    fixed: int,
   121→    resolved: int,
   122→    skip_reasons: dict[str, int] | None = None,
   123→):
   124→    skipped = detected - fixed
   125→    print(colorize("\n  ── Post-fix check ──", "dim"))
   126→    print(
   127→        colorize(
   128→            f"  Fixed {fixed}/{detected} ({skipped} skipped, {resolved} issues resolved)",
   129→            "dim",
   130→        )
   131→    )
   132→    if skip_reasons and skipped > 0:
   133→        print(colorize(f"\n  Skip reasons ({skipped} total):", "dim"))
   134→        for reason, count in sorted(skip_reasons.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1]):
   135→            print(
   136→                colorize(
   137→                    f"    {count:4d}  {_SKIP_REASON_LABELS.get(reason, reason)}", "dim"
   138→                )
   139→            )
   140→        print()
   141→
   142→    checklist = [
   143→        "Run your language typecheck/build command — does it still build?",
   144→        "Spot-check a few changed files — do the edits look correct?",
   145→    ]
   146→    if skipped > 0 and not skip_reasons:
   147→        checklist.append(
   148→            f"{skipped} items were skipped. Should the fixer handle more patterns?"
   149→        )
   150→    checklist += [
   151→        "Run `desloppify scan` to update state and refresh issues.",
   152→        "Are there cascading effects? (e.g., removing vars may orphan imports)",
   153→        "`git diff --stat` — review before committing. Anything surprising?",
   154→    ]
   155→    print(colorize("  Checklist:", "dim"))
   156→    for index, item in enumerate(checklist, 1):
   157→        print(colorize(f"  {index}. {item}", "dim"))
   158→
   159→
   160→__all__ = [
   161→    "_cascade_unused_import_cleanup",
   162→    "_print_fix_retro",
   163→    "_resolve_fixer_results",
   164→    "_warn_uncommitted_changes",
   165→]
   166→

1→"""Detect repeated code blocks via jscpd.
     2→
     3→Replaces boilerplate_duplication.py with a thin adapter around jscpd
     4→(https://github.com/kucherenko/jscpd), which uses proper per-language
     5→tokenisation and supports type-2 clones (renamed identifiers).
     6→
     7→Falls back gracefully to None when jscpd/npx is not installed.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from __future__ import annotations
    11→
    12→import hashlib
    13→import json
    14→import logging
    15→import shutil
    16→import subprocess
    17→import tempfile
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→
    20→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import (
    21→
    22→    collect_exclude_dirs,
    23→
    24→    get_exclusions,
    25→
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import warn_best_effort
    28→
    29→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    30→
    31→_BASE_IGNORES = (
    32→    "**/node_modules/**",
    33→    "**/.git/**",
    34→    "**/__pycache__/**",
    35→    "**/.venv*/**",
    36→    "**/venv/**",
    37→    "**/.desloppify/**",
    38→    "**/.claude/**",
    39→)
    40→
    41→_ARTIFACT_PREFIXES = ("build/", "dist/", ".desloppify/", ".claude/")
    42→_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX = "build/lib/"
    43→
    44→
    45→def _to_scan_relative(path_resolved: Path, name: str) -> str | None:
    46→    """Return scan-relative path, or None when file is outside scan path."""
    47→    if not name:
    48→        return None
    49→    try:
    50→        rel = Path(name).resolve().relative_to(path_resolved)
    51→    except (ValueError, OSError):
    52→        return None
    53→    return str(rel).replace("\\", "/")
    54→
    55→
    56→def _is_artifact_path(rel_path: str) -> bool:
    57→    return rel_path.startswith(_ARTIFACT_PREFIXES)
    58→
    59→
    60→def _is_build_mirror_pair(first_rel: str, second_rel: str) -> bool:
    61→    if first_rel.startswith(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):
    62→        return first_rel[len(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):] == second_rel
    63→    if second_rel.startswith(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):
    64→        return second_rel[len(_BUILD_MIRROR_PREFIX):] == first_rel
    65→    return False
    66→
    67→
    68→def _as_jscpd_globs(pattern: str) -> list[str]:
    69→    raw = pattern.strip().replace("\\", "/").strip("/")
    70→    if not raw:
    71→        return []
    72→    globs = [f"**/{raw}/**"]
    73→    basename = raw.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
    74→    if "." in basename or "*" in basename:
    75→        globs.append(f"**/{raw}")
    76→    return globs
    77→
    78→
    79→def _jscpd_ignore_arg(scan_path: Path) -> str:
    80→    """Build jscpd ignore globs from defaults + runtime excludes."""
    81→    patterns = set(_BASE_IGNORES)
    82→    # Use collect_exclude_dirs for the directory-name portion, then convert
    83→    # the remaining runtime globs (patterns with '*') to jscpd format.
    84→    for dirname in collect_exclude_dirs(scan_path):
    85→        base = Path(dirname).name
    86→        patterns.update(_as_jscpd_globs(base))
    87→    for pattern in get_exclusions():
    88→        if "*" in pattern:
    89→            patterns.update(_as_jscpd_globs(pattern))
    90→    return ",".join(sorted(patterns))
    91→
    92→
    93→def _parse_jscpd_report(report: dict, scan_path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    94→    """Parse a jscpd JSON report dict and return clustered duplication entries.
    95→
    96→    Clusters pairwise duplicates that share the same fragment into multi-file
    97→    groups, producing the same output shape as the old boilerplate_duplication
    98→    detector so that phase_boilerplate_duplication() needs no further changes.
    99→    """
   100→    duplicates = report.get("duplicates", [])
   101→    if not duplicates:
   102→        return []
   103→
   104→    path_resolved = scan_path.resolve()
   105→
   106→    # Cluster pairs by SHA256(fragment.strip()[:200])[:16]
   107→    clusters: dict[str, dict] = {}
   108→
   109→    for dup in duplicates:
   110→        fragment = dup.get("fragment", "")
   111→        fragment_key = hashlib.sha256(
   112→            fragment.strip()[:200].encode("utf-8", errors="replace")
   113→        ).hexdigest()[:16]
   114→
   115→        first = dup.get("firstFile", {})
   116→        second = dup.get("secondFile", {})
   117→        first_name = first.get("name", "")
   118→        second_name = second.get("name", "")
   119→
   120→        first_rel = _to_scan_relative(path_resolved, first_name)
   121→        second_rel = _to_scan_relative(path_resolved, second_name)
   122→        if first_rel is None or second_rel is None:
   123→            continue
   124→        if (
   125→            first_rel == second_rel
   126→            or _is_artifact_path(first_rel)
   127→            or _is_artifact_path(second_rel)
   128→            or _is_build_mirror_pair(first_rel, second_rel)
   129→        ):
   130→            continue
   131→
   132→        lines = dup.get("lines", 0)
   133→        if fragment_key not in clusters:
   134→            clusters[fragment_key] = {
   135→                "id": fragment_key,
   136→                "lines": lines,
   137→                "fragment": fragment,
   138→                "files": {},
   139→            }
   140→
   141→        cluster = clusters[fragment_key]
   142→        for name, info in [(first_rel, first), (second_rel, second)]:
   143→            if name not in cluster["files"]:
   144→                cluster["files"][name] = info.get("start", 0)
   145→
   146→    entries: list[dict] = []
   147→    for cluster in clusters.values():
   148→        files = cluster["files"]
   149→        if len(files) < 2:
   150→            continue
   151→        locations = [
   152→            {"file": f, "line": line}
   153→            for f, line in sorted(files.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[0])
   154→        ]
   155→        entries.append(
   156→            {
   157→                "id": cluster["id"],
   158→                "distinct_files": len(files),
   159→                "window_size": cluster["lines"],
   160→                "locations": locations,
   161→                "sample": cluster["fragment"].splitlines()[:4],
   162→            }
   163→        )
   164→
   165→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["distinct_files"], e["id"]))
   166→    return entries
   167→
   168→
   169→def _resolve_jscpd_command() -> list[str] | None:
   170→    """Return the best available command prefix for running jscpd.
   171→
   172→    Prefers a locally/globally installed ``jscpd`` executable over ``npx``.
   173→    Returns ``None`` when neither ``jscpd`` nor ``npx`` can be found.
   174→    """
   175→    jscpd_path = shutil.which("jscpd")
   176→    if jscpd_path:
   177→        logger.debug("jscpd: using installed executable at %s", jscpd_path)
   178→        return [jscpd_path]
   179→    npx_path = shutil.which("npx")
   180→    if npx_path:
   181→        logger.debug("jscpd: falling back to npx at %s", npx_path)
   182→        return [npx_path, "--yes", "jscpd"]
   183→    return None
   184→
   185→
   186→def detect_with_jscpd(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
   187→    """Run jscpd on *path* and return duplication entries, or None on failure."""
   188→    cmd_prefix = _resolve_jscpd_command()
   189→    if cmd_prefix is None:
   190→        warn_best_effort(
   191→            "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd/npx not found. "
   192→            "Install with `npm install -g jscpd`."
   193→        )
   194→        logger.debug("jscpd: neither jscpd nor npx found — skipping")
   195→        return None
   196→
   197→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   198→        try:
   199→            # Static jscpd argv only; command prefix is resolved before execution.
   200→            subprocess.run(
   201→                [
   202→                    *cmd_prefix,
   203→                    str(path),
   204→                    "--reporters",
   205→                    "json",
   206→                    "--output",
   207→                    tmpdir,
   208→                    "--min-lines",
   209→                    "4",
   210→                    "--min-tokens",
   211→                    "50",
   212→                    "--ignore",
   213→                    _jscpd_ignore_arg(path),
   214→                    "--silent",
   215→                ],
   216→                capture_output=True,
   217→                text=True,
   218→                timeout=120,
   219→                check=True,
   220→                shell=False,  # nosec B603
   221→            )
   222→        except FileNotFoundError:
   223→            warn_best_effort(
   224→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd/npx not found. "
   225→                "Install with `npm install -g jscpd`."
   226→            )
   227→            logger.debug("jscpd: command not found at runtime — skipping")
   228→            return None
   229→        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as exc:
   230→            warn_best_effort(
   231→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd exited with errors."
   232→            )
   233→            logger.debug(
   234→                "jscpd: non-zero exit (%s): %s",
   235→                exc.returncode,
   236→                (exc.stderr or "").strip(),
   237→            )
   238→            return None
   239→        except OSError as exc:
   240→            warn_best_effort(
   241→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd failed to run."
   242→            )
   243→            logger.debug("jscpd: OS error running jscpd: %s", exc)
   244→            return None
   245→        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   246→            warn_best_effort(
   247→                "Boilerplate duplication detection skipped: jscpd timed out after 120s."
   248→            )
   249→            logger.debug("jscpd: timed out")
   250→            return None
   251→
   252→        report_file = Path(tmpdir) / "jscpd-report.json"
   253→        if not report_file.exists():
   254→            logger.debug("jscpd: no report file produced — assuming no duplicates")
   255→            return []
   256→
   257→        try:
   258→            report = json.loads(report_file.read_text())
   259→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   260→            logger.debug("jscpd: failed to parse report: %s", exc)
   261→            return None
   262→
   263→        return _parse_jscpd_report(report, path)
   264→
   265→
   266→__all__ = ["_parse_jscpd_report", "_resolve_jscpd_command", "detect_with_jscpd"]
   267→
```

1→"""Detect architectural layer violations via import-linter.
     2→
     3→Reads `.importlinter` config from the scanned project's root (walking up
     4→from the scan path to find the first `.importlinter` file, stopping at `.git`
     5→boundaries). Falls back gracefully to None when lint-imports is not installed
     6→or no config is found.
     7→
     8→See https://import-linter.readthedocs.io for config format.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import re
    15→import subprocess
    16→from pathlib import Path
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→_BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE = re.compile(r"Broken contract '([^']+)'")
    21→# import-linter outputs violations as "  module.a imports module.b"
    22→_VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+imports\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    23→# Also handle "  module.a -> module.b" format (arrow notation)
    24→_VIOLATION_ARROW_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+->\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    25→
    26→
    27→def _find_importlinter_root(path: Path) -> Path | None:
    28→    """Walk up from path to find the directory containing .importlinter.
    29→
    30→    Stops when a .git boundary or the filesystem root is encountered.
    31→    """
    32→    candidate = path.resolve()
    33→    for directory in [candidate, *candidate.parents]:
    34→        if (directory / ".importlinter").exists():
    35→            return directory
    36→        # Stop at project root boundaries
    37→        if (directory / ".git").exists() or directory.parent == directory:
    38→            break
    39→    return None
    40→
    41→
    42→def _module_to_file(module: str) -> str:
    43→    """Convert dotted module name to a relative file path."""
    44→    return module.replace(".", "/") + ".py"
    45→
    46→
    47→def detect_with_import_linter(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    48→    """Run lint-imports and return layer violation entries, or None on failure."""
    49→    config_dir = _find_importlinter_root(path)
    50→    if config_dir is None:
    51→        logger.debug("import-linter: no .importlinter config found — skipping")
    52→        return None
    53→
    54→    try:
    55→        # Static lint-imports argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    56→        result = subprocess.run(
    57→            [_resolve_cli_executable("lint-imports")],
    58→            capture_output=True,
    59→            text=True,
    60→            cwd=config_dir,
    61→            timeout=60,
    62→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    63→        )
    64→    except FileNotFoundError:
    65→        logger.debug("import-linter: lint-imports not found — skipping")
    66→        return None
    67→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    68→        logger.debug("import-linter: timed out")
    69→        return None
    70→
    71→    if result.returncode == 0:
    72→        return []
    73→
    74→    output = result.stdout + result.stderr
    75→    if not output.strip():
    76→        return []
    77→
    78→    entries: list[dict] = []
    79→    current_contract: str | None = None
    80→
    81→    for line in output.splitlines():
    82→        m_contract = _BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE.search(line)
    83→        if m_contract:
    84→            current_contract = m_contract.group(1)
    85→            continue
    86→
    87→        if current_contract is None:
    88→            continue
    89→
    90→        m_viol = _VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE.match(line) or _VIOLATION_ARROW_RE.match(line)
    91→        if not m_viol:
    92→            continue
    93→
    94→        source_mod = m_viol.group(1)
    95→        target_mod = m_viol.group(2)
    96→        source_file = _module_to_file(source_mod)
    97→        source_pkg = source_mod.rsplit(".", 1)[-1] if "." in source_mod else source_mod
    98→
    99→        entries.append(
   100→            {
   101→                "file": source_file,
   102→                "line": 0,
   103→                "summary": (
   104→                    f"Layer violation: {source_mod} -> {target_mod}"
   105→                    f" (contract: {current_contract})"
   106→                ),
   107→                "confidence": "high",
   108→                "source_pkg": source_pkg,
   109→                "target_pkg": target_mod,
   110→            }
   111→        )
   112→
   113→    return entries
   114→
   115→
   116→__all__ = ["detect_with_import_linter"]
   117→
```

1→"""Python unused detection via ruff (F401=unused imports, F841=unused vars)."""
     2→
     3→import json
     4→import re
     5→import subprocess
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import collect_exclude_dirs as _collect_exclude_dirs
     9→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_py_files
    10→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import get_exclusions as _get_exclusions
    11→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion as _matches_exclusion
    12→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    13→
    14→
    15→def _selected_codes(category: str) -> list[str]:
    16→    select = []
    17→    if category in ("all", "imports"):
    18→        select.append("F401")
    19→    if category in ("all", "vars"):
    20→        select.append("F841")
    21→    return select
    22→
    23→
    24→def _is_excluded(filepath: str, exclusions: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
    25→    return bool(
    26→        exclusions
    27→        and any(_matches_exclusion(filepath, ex) for ex in exclusions)
    28→    )
    29→
    30→
    31→def _extract_unused_name(message: str, *, name_re: re.Pattern[str]) -> str:
    32→    match = name_re.search(message)
    33→    return match.group(1) if match else message.split()[0]
    34→
    35→
    36→def _parse_ruff_diagnostics(
    37→    diagnostics: list[dict],
    38→    *,
    39→    category: str,
    40→    exclusions: tuple[str, ...],
    41→) -> list[dict]:
    42→    entries = []
    43→    name_re = re.compile(r"`([^`]+)`")
    44→    for diagnostic in diagnostics:
    45→        code = diagnostic.get("code", "")
    46→        filepath = diagnostic.get("filename", "")
    47→        if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        # F401 in __init__.py = re-export, not dead code
    51→        if code == "F401" and filepath.endswith("__init__.py"):
    52→            continue
    53→
    54→        cat = "imports" if code == "F401" else "vars"
    55→        if category != "all" and cat != category:
    56→            continue
    57→
    58→        message = diagnostic.get("message", "")
    59→        name = _extract_unused_name(message, name_re=name_re)
    60→        if name.startswith("_"):
    61→            continue
    62→
    63→        location = diagnostic.get("location", {})
    64→        entries.append(
    65→            {
    66→                "file": filepath,
    67→                "line": location.get("row", 0),
    68→                "col": location.get("column", 0),
    69→                "name": name,
    70→                "category": cat,
    71→            }
    72→        )
    73→    return entries
    74→
    75→
    76→def _parse_pyflakes_lines(
    77→    lines: list[str],
    78→    *,
    79→    category: str,
    80→    exclusions: tuple[str, ...],
    81→) -> list[dict]:
    82→    entries = []
    83→    import_re = re.compile(r"^(.+):(\d+):\d*\s+'([^']+)'\s+imported but unused")
    84→    var_re = re.compile(
    85→        r"^(.+):(\d+):\d*\s+local variable '([^']+)' is assigned to but never used"
    86→    )
    87→
    88→    for line in lines:
    89→        import_match = import_re.match(line)
    90→        if import_match and category in ("all", "imports"):
    91→            filepath = import_match.group(1)
    92→            if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
    93→                continue
    94→            # F401 in __init__.py = re-export, not dead code
    95→            if filepath.endswith("__init__.py"):
    96→                continue
    97→            entries.append(
    98→                {
    99→                    "file": filepath,
   100→                    "line": int(import_match.group(2)),
   101→                    "col": 0,
   102→                    "name": import_match.group(3),
   103→                    "category": "imports",
   104→                }
   105→            )
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        var_match = var_re.match(line)
   109→        if var_match and category in ("all", "vars"):
   110→            filepath = var_match.group(1)
   111→            if _is_excluded(filepath, exclusions):
   112→                continue
   113→            entries.append(
   114→                {
   115→                    "file": filepath,
   116→                    "line": int(var_match.group(2)),
   117→                    "col": 0,
   118→                    "name": var_match.group(3),
   119→                    "category": "vars",
   120→                }
   121→            )
   122→    return entries
   123→
   124→
   125→def detect_unused(path: Path, category: str = "all") -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   126→    """Detect unused imports and variables using ruff.
   127→
   128→    Falls back to pyflakes if ruff is not available.
   129→    Returns (entries, total_statements_checked).
   130→    """
   131→    total_files = len(find_py_files(path))
   132→
   133→    entries = _try_ruff(path, category)
   134→    if entries is not None:
   135→        return entries, total_files
   136→
   137→    entries = _try_pyflakes(path, category)
   138→    if entries is not None:
   139→        return entries, total_files
   140→
   141→    return [], total_files
   142→
   143→
   144→def _try_ruff(path: Path, category: str) -> list[dict] | None:
   145→    """Try ruff for unused detection."""
   146→    select = _selected_codes(category)
   147→    if not select:
   148→        return []
   149→
   150→    exclude_dirs = _collect_exclude_dirs(path)
   151→    cmd = [
   152→        "ruff",
   153→        "check",
   154→        "--select",
   155→        ",".join(select),
   156→        "--output-format",
   157→        "json",
   158→        "--no-fix",
   159→    ]
   160→    if exclude_dirs:
   161→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
   162→    cmd.append(str(path))
   163→
   164→    try:
   165→        # Static ruff argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   166→        result = subprocess.run(
   167→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
   168→            capture_output=True,
   169→            text=True,
   170→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   171→            timeout=60,
   172→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   173→        )
   174→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   175→        return None
   176→
   177→    if not result.stdout.strip():
   178→        return []
   179→
   180→    try:
   181→        diagnostics = json.loads(result.stdout)
   182→    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   183→        return None
   184→
   185→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   186→    return _parse_ruff_diagnostics(
   187→        diagnostics, category=category, exclusions=exclusions
   188→    )
   189→
   190→
   191→def _try_pyflakes(path: Path, category: str) -> list[dict] | None:
   192→    """Fallback: try pyflakes for unused detection."""
   193→    try:
   194→        # Static pyflakes argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   195→        result = subprocess.run(
   196→            [_resolve_cli_executable("pyflakes"), str(path)],
   197→            capture_output=True,
   198→            text=True,
   199→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   200→            timeout=60,
   201→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   202→        )
   203→    except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   204→        return None
   205→
   206→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   207→    lines = (result.stdout + result.stderr).splitlines()
   208→    return _parse_pyflakes_lines(lines, category=category, exclusions=exclusions)
   209→
```
