1→"""Bandit adapter — Python security detection via the bandit static analyser.
     2→
     3→Runs ``bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>`` as a subprocess and converts its JSON
     4→output into the security entry dicts expected by ``phase_security``.
     5→
     6→Bandit covers AST-level security checks (shell injection, unsafe deserialization,
     7→SQL injection, etc.) more reliably than custom regex/AST patterns. When bandit is
     8→installed, it is used as the lang-specific security detector; otherwise
     9→Python-specific security checks will be skipped.
    10→
    11→Bandit severity → desloppify tier/confidence mapping:
    12→  HIGH   → tier=4, confidence="high"
    13→  MEDIUM → tier=3, confidence="medium"
    14→  LOW    → tier=3, confidence="low"
    15→
    16→The ``check_id`` in the entry detail is the bandit test ID (e.g., "B602") so
    17→issues are stable across reruns and can be wontfix-tracked by ID.
    18→"""
    19→
    20→from __future__ import annotations
    21→
    22→import json
    23→import logging
    24→import subprocess
    25→from dataclasses import dataclass
    26→from pathlib import Path
    27→from typing import Literal
    28→
    29→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    30→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    31→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap, Zone
    32→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import DetectorCoverageStatus
    33→
    34→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    35→
    36→_SEVERITY_TO_TIER = {"HIGH": 4, "MEDIUM": 3, "LOW": 3}
    37→_SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE = {"HIGH": "high", "MEDIUM": "medium", "LOW": "low"}
    38→
    39→# Bandit test IDs that overlap with the cross-language security detector
    40→# (secret names, hardcoded passwords). Skip these to avoid duplicate issues.
    41→_CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP = frozenset(
    42→    {
    43→        "B105",  # hardcoded_password_string
    44→        "B106",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    45→        "B107",  # hardcoded_[REDACTED]
    46→        "B501",  # request_with_no_cert_validation  (covered by weak_crypto_tls)
    47→        "B502",  # ssl_with_bad_version
    48→        "B503",  # ssl_with_bad_defaults
    49→        "B504",  # ssl_with_no_version
    50→        "B505",  # weak_cryptographic_key
    51→    }
    52→)
    53→
    54→_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT = (
    55→    "Python-specific security checks were skipped; this can miss shell injection, "
    56→    "unsafe deserialization, and risky SQL/subprocess patterns."
    57→)
    58→
    59→
    60→BanditRunState = Literal["ok", "missing_tool", "timeout", "error", "parse_error"]
    61→
    62→
    63→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    64→class BanditRunStatus:
    65→    """Typed execution status for a Bandit adapter invocation."""
    66→
    67→    state: BanditRunState
    68→    detail: str = ""
    69→    tool: str = "bandit"
    70→
    71→    def coverage(self) -> DetectorCoverageStatus | None:
    72→        """Convert non-success statuses into detector coverage metadata."""
    73→        if self.state == "ok":
    74→            return None
    75→
    76→        if self.state == "missing_tool":
    77→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    78→                detector="security",
    79→                status="reduced",
    80→                confidence=0.6,
    81→                summary="bandit is not installed — Python-specific security checks were skipped.",
    82→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
    83→                remediation="Install Bandit: pip install bandit",
    84→                tool=self.tool,
    85→                reason="missing_dependency",
    86→            )
    87→
    88→        if self.state == "timeout":
    89→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
    90→                detector="security",
    91→                status="reduced",
    92→                confidence=0.75,
    93→                summary="bandit timed out — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
    94→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
    95→                remediation="Rerun scan or run `bandit -r -f json --quiet <path>` manually.",
    96→                tool=self.tool,
    97→                reason="timeout",
    98→            )
    99→
   100→        if self.state == "parse_error":
   101→            return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   102→                detector="security",
   103→                status="reduced",
   104→                confidence=0.75,
   105→                summary="bandit output could not be parsed — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   106→                impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   107→                remediation="Update/reinstall Bandit and rerun scan.",
   108→                tool=self.tool,
   109→                reason="parse_error",
   110→            )
   111→
   112→        return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   113→            detector="security",
   114→            status="reduced",
   115→            confidence=0.75,
   116→            summary="bandit failed to execute — Python-specific security checks were skipped this scan.",
   117→            impact=_BANDIT_IMPACT_TEXT,
   118→            remediation="Verify Bandit is runnable and rerun scan.",
   119→            tool=self.tool,
   120→            reason="execution_error",
   121→        )
   122→
   123→
   124→@dataclass(frozen=True)
   125→class BanditScanResult:
   126→    """Bandit issues plus typed execution status."""
   127→
   128→    entries: list[dict]
   129→    files_scanned: int
   130→    status: BanditRunStatus
   131→
   132→
   133→def _to_security_entry(
   134→    result: dict,
   135→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   136→) -> dict | None:
   137→    """Convert a single bandit result dict to a security entry, or None to skip."""
   138→    filepath = str(result.get("filename", "") or "")
   139→    if not filepath:
   140→        return None
   141→
   142→    rel_path = rel(filepath)
   143→
   144→    # Apply zone filtering — only GENERATED and VENDOR are excluded for security.
   145→    if zone_map is not None:
   146→        zone = zone_map.get(rel_path)
   147→        if zone in (Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR):
   148→            return None
   149→
   150→    test_id = result.get("test_id", "")
   151→    if test_id in _CROSS_LANG_OVERLAP:
   152→        return None
   153→
   154→    raw_severity = result.get("issue_severity", "MEDIUM").upper()
   155→    raw_confidence = result.get("issue_confidence", "MEDIUM").upper()
   156→
   157→    # Suppress LOW-severity + LOW-confidence (very noisy, low signal).
   158→    if raw_severity == "LOW" and raw_confidence == "LOW":
   159→        return None
   160→
   161→    tier = _SEVERITY_TO_TIER.get(raw_severity, 3)
   162→    confidence = _SEVERITY_TO_CONFIDENCE.get(raw_severity, "medium")
   163→
   164→    line = result.get("line_number", 0)
   165→    summary = result.get("issue_text", "")
   166→    test_name = result.get("test_name", test_id)
   167→    return {
   168→        "file": rel_path,
   169→        "name": f"security::{test_id}::{rel_path}::{line}",
   170→        "tier": tier,
   171→        "confidence": confidence,
   172→        "summary": f"[{test_id}] {summary}",
   173→        "detail": {
   174→            "kind": test_id,
   175→            "severity": raw_severity.lower(),
   176→            "line": line,
   177→            "content": result.get("code", "")[:200],
   178→            "remediation": result.get("more_info", ""),
   179→            "test_name": test_name,
   180→            "source": "bandit",
   181→        },
   182→    }
   183→
   184→
   185→def detect_with_bandit(
   186→    path: Path,
   187→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   188→    timeout: int = 120,
   189→    exclude_dirs: list[str] | None = None,
   190→) -> BanditScanResult:
   191→    """Run bandit on *path* and return issues + typed execution status.
   192→
   193→    Parameters
   194→    ----------
   195→    exclude_dirs:
   196→        Absolute directory paths to pass to bandit's ``--exclude`` flag.
   197→        When non-empty, bandit will skip these directories during its
   198→        recursive scan.
   199→    """
   200→    cmd = [
   201→        "bandit",
   202→        "-r",
   203→        "-f",
   204→        "json",
   205→        "--quiet",
   206→    ]
   207→    if exclude_dirs:
   208→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
   209→    cmd.append(str(path.resolve()))
   210→
   211→    try:
   212→        # Static bandit argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
   213→        result = subprocess.run(
   214→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
   215→            capture_output=True,
   216→            text=True,
   217→            cwd=get_project_root(),
   218→            timeout=timeout,
   219→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
   220→        )
   221→    except FileNotFoundError:
   222→        logger.debug("bandit: not installed — Python-specific security checks will be skipped")
   223→        return BanditScanResult(
   224→            entries=[],
   225→            files_scanned=0,
   226→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="missing_tool"),
   227→        )
   228→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   229→        logger.debug("bandit: timed out after %ds", timeout)
   230→        return BanditScanResult(
   231→            entries=[],
   232→            files_scanned=0,
   233→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="timeout", detail=f"timeout={timeout}s"),
   234→        )
   235→    except OSError as exc:
   236→        logger.debug("bandit: OSError: %s", exc)
   237→        return BanditScanResult(
   238→            entries=[],
   239→            files_scanned=0,
   240→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="error", detail=str(exc)),
   241→        )
   242→
   243→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   244→    if not stdout:
   245→        # Bandit exits 0 with no output when there's nothing to scan.
   246→        return BanditScanResult(
   247→            entries=[],
   248→            files_scanned=0,
   249→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),
   250→        )
   251→
   252→    try:
   253→        data = json.loads(stdout)
   254→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   255→        logger.debug("bandit: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   256→        return BanditScanResult(
   257→            entries=[],
   258→            files_scanned=0,
   259→            status=BanditRunStatus(state="parse_error", detail=str(exc)),
   260→        )
   261→
   262→    raw_results: list[dict] = data.get("results", [])
   263→    metrics: dict = data.get("metrics", {})
   264→
   265→    # Count scanned files from metrics (bandit reports per-file stats).
   266→    files_scanned = sum(
   267→        1
   268→        for key in metrics
   269→        if key != "_totals" and not key.endswith("_totals")
   270→    )
   271→
   272→    entries: list[dict] = []
   273→    for res in raw_results:
   274→        entry = _to_security_entry(res, zone_map)
   275→        if entry is not None:
   276→            entries.append(entry)
   277→
   278→    logger.debug("bandit: %d issues from %d files", len(entries), files_scanned)
   279→    return BanditScanResult(
   280→        entries=entries,
   281→        files_scanned=files_scanned,
   282→        status=BanditRunStatus(state="ok"),
   283→    )
   284→
```

1→"""Python smell detection via ruff (B/BLE/E/F/PGH/PLW/RUF/W-series rules).
     2→
     3→Runs ``ruff check`` with a targeted rule set and maps each ruff code to a
     4→desloppify smell ID. Produces smell entries in the same format as detect_smells()
     5→so they flow through make_smell_issues() without any plumbing changes.
     6→
     7→Falls back gracefully to [] when ruff is not installed.
     8→
     9→Rules (non-overlapping with remaining custom smells in smells.py):
    10→  B006     – mutable default argument (replaces regex in smells.py)
    11→  B007     – unused loop control variable
    12→  B023     – function definition does not bind loop variable
    13→  B026     – star-arg after keyword argument
    14→  B904     – raise inside except without ``from err``
    15→  BLE001   – blind exception catch (replaces broad_except regex)
    16→  E711     – comparison to None with == / !=
    17→  E712     – comparison to True/False with == / !=
    18→  E722     – bare except (replaces bare_except regex)
    19→  F403     – star import (replaces star_import regex; keep star_import_no_all AST)
    20→  PGH003   – blanket type: ignore (replaces type_ignore regex)
    21→  PLW0603  – global keyword (replaces global_keyword regex)
    22→  RUF012   – mutable class variable (replaces AST mutable_class_var)
    23→  W605     – invalid escape sequence (e.g. "\\d" instead of r"\\d")
    24→"""
    25→
    26→from __future__ import annotations
    27→
    28→import json
    29→import logging
    30→import subprocess
    31→from collections import defaultdict
    32→from pathlib import Path
    33→
    34→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import collect_exclude_dirs as _collect_exclude_dirs
    35→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import get_exclusions as _get_exclusions
    36→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import matches_exclusion as _matches_exclusion
    37→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    38→
    39→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    40→
    41→# Maps ruff code → (smell_id, label, severity)
    42→_RULE_MAP: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] = {
    43→    "B006": ("mutable_default", "Mutable default argument (list/dict/set literal)", "high"),
    44→    "B007": ("unused_loop_var", "Unused loop control variable", "medium"),
    45→    "B023": ("func_in_loop", "Function definition doesn't bind loop variable", "high"),
    46→    "B026": ("star_after_keyword", "Star-arg unpacking after keyword argument", "high"),
    47→    "B904": ("raise_without_from", "Raise inside except without `from err`", "medium"),
    48→    "BLE001": ("broad_except", "Broad except — check library exceptions before narrowing", "medium"),
    49→    "E711": ("none_comparison", "Comparison to None with == / !=  (use `is`)", "medium"),
    50→    "E712": ("bool_comparison", "Comparison to True/False with == / != (use `is`)", "low"),
    51→    "E722": ("bare_except", "Bare except clause (catches everything including SystemExit)", "high"),
    52→    "F403": ("star_import", "Star import (from X import *)", "medium"),
    53→    "PGH003": ("type_ignore", "type: ignore comment", "medium"),
    54→    "PLW0603": ("global_keyword", "Global keyword usage", "medium"),
    55→    "RUF012": ("mutable_class_var", "Class-level mutable default (shared across instances)", "high"),
    56→    "W605": ("invalid_escape", "Invalid escape sequence (use raw string or \\\\)", "medium"),
    57→}
    58→
    59→_SELECT = ",".join(_RULE_MAP)
    60→
    61→
    62→def detect_with_ruff_smells(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    63→    """Run ruff on supplemental B/E/W rules and return smell entries, or None on failure.
    64→
    65→    Each entry matches the format expected by make_smell_issues():
    66→        {
    67→            "id": smell_id,
    68→            "label": label,
    69→            "severity": "high"|"medium"|"low",
    70→            "matches": [{"file": str, "line": int}, ...],
    71→        }
    72→    """
    73→    exclude_dirs = _collect_exclude_dirs(path)
    74→    cmd = [
    75→        "ruff",
    76→        "check",
    77→        "--select",
    78→        _SELECT,
    79→        "--output-format",
    80→        "json",
    81→        "--no-fix",
    82→    ]
    83→    if exclude_dirs:
    84→        cmd.extend(["--exclude", ",".join(exclude_dirs)])
    85→    cmd.append(str(path))
    86→
    87→    try:
    88→        # Static ruff argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    89→        result = subprocess.run(
    90→            [_resolve_cli_executable(cmd[0]), *cmd[1:]],
    91→            capture_output=True,
    92→            text=True,
    93→            cwd=get_project_root(),
    94→            timeout=60,
    95→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    96→        )
    97→    except FileNotFoundError:
    98→        logger.debug("ruff smells: ruff not found — skipping supplemental smell detection")
    99→        return None
   100→    except OSError as exc:
   101→        logger.debug("ruff smells: OS error running ruff: %s", exc)
   102→        return None
   103→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   104→        logger.debug("ruff smells: timed out")
   105→        return None
   106→
   107→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
   108→    if not stdout:
   109→        return []
   110→
   111→    try:
   112→        diagnostics: list[dict] = json.loads(stdout)
   113→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   114→        logger.debug("ruff smells: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
   115→        return None
   116→
   117→    exclusions = _get_exclusions()
   118→
   119→    # Group diagnostics by (code, file) → list of matches.
   120→    # Then convert to smell entry format.
   121→    by_code_file: dict[tuple[str, str], list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
   122→    for diag in diagnostics:
   123→        code = diag.get("code", "")
   124→        if code not in _RULE_MAP:
   125→            continue
   126→        filepath = diag.get("filename", "")
   127→        if not filepath:
   128→            continue
   129→        if exclusions and any(_matches_exclusion(filepath, ex) for ex in exclusions):
   130→            continue
   131→        location = diag.get("location", {})
   132→        line = location.get("row", 0) if isinstance(location, dict) else 0
   133→        by_code_file[(code, filepath)].append({"file": filepath, "line": line})
   134→
   135→    # Aggregate by code across all files.
   136→    by_code: dict[str, dict[str, list[dict]]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
   137→    for (code, filepath), matches in by_code_file.items():
   138→        by_code[code][filepath].extend(matches)
   139→
   140→    entries: list[dict] = []
   141→    for code, files in by_code.items():
   142→        smell_id, label, severity = _RULE_MAP[code]
   143→        all_matches = [m for ms in files.values() for m in ms]
   144→        entries.append(
   145→            {
   146→                "id": smell_id,
   147→                "label": label,
   148→                "severity": severity,
   149→                "matches": all_matches,
   150→            }
   151→        )
   152→
   153→    logger.debug("ruff smells: %d supplemental smell entries", len(entries))
   154→    return entries
   155→

1→"""Knip adapter — dead exports detection via Knip.
     2→
     3→Runs ``npx knip --reporter json`` as a subprocess and parses its JSON output
     4→into the entry dicts expected by detect_dead_exports().
     5→
     6→Knip understands re-exports, barrel files, dynamic imports, and entry points —
     7→far more accurate than the old grep-based approach.
     8→
     9→Returns None when Knip is not installed, so the caller gets [] issues rather
    10→than a crash.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import json
    16→import logging
    17→import subprocess
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→
    20→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→
    25→def _run_knip(path: Path, timeout: int = 120) -> dict | None:
    26→    """Run ``npx knip --reporter json`` and return parsed JSON, or None on failure."""
    27→    try:
    28→        # Static knip argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    29→        result = subprocess.run(
    30→            [
    31→                _resolve_cli_executable("npx"),
    32→                "knip",
    33→                "--reporter",
    34→                "json",
    35→                "--no-gitignore",
    36→            ],
    37→            capture_output=True,
    38→            text=True,
    39→            cwd=str(path),
    40→            timeout=timeout,
    41→            shell=False,  # nosec B603
    42→        )
    43→    except FileNotFoundError:
    44→        logger.debug("knip: npx not found")
    45→        return None
    46→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    47→        logger.debug("knip: timed out after %ds", timeout)
    48→        return None
    49→    except OSError as exc:
    50→        logger.debug("knip: OSError: %s", exc)
    51→        return None
    52→
    53→    stdout = result.stdout.strip()
    54→    if not stdout:
    55→        return None
    56→
    57→    try:
    58→        return json.loads(stdout)
    59→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    60→        logger.debug("knip: JSON parse error: %s", exc)
    61→        return None
    62→
    63→
    64→def _normalize_path(raw: str, scan_path: Path) -> str:
    65→    """Return a path relative to PROJECT_ROOT, scoped to scan_path."""
    66→    p = Path(raw)
    67→    if not p.is_absolute():
    68→        p = (scan_path / p).resolve()
    69→    try:
    70→        p.relative_to(scan_path.resolve())
    71→    except ValueError:
    72→        return ""
    73→    return rel(str(p))
    74→
    75→
    76→def detect_with_knip(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    77→    """Run Knip and return export entries, or None if Knip is unavailable.
    78→
    79→    Each entry: {"file": str, "name": str, "line": int, "kind": "export"|"type"}
    80→    """
    81→    data = _run_knip(path)
    82→    if data is None:
    83→        return None
    84→
    85→    entries: list[dict] = []
    86→
    87→    for issue in data.get("issues", []):
    88→        raw_file = issue.get("file", "")
    89→        norm = _normalize_path(raw_file, path)
    90→        if not norm:
    91→            continue
    92→
    93→        for export in issue.get("exports", []):
    94→            name = export.get("name", "")
    95→            if not name:
    96→                continue
    97→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
    98→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
    99→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   100→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   101→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   102→                line = raw_pos
   103→            else:
   104→                line = 0
   105→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "export"})
   106→
   107→        for export in issue.get("types", []):
   108→            name = export.get("name", "")
   109→            if not name:
   110→                continue
   111→            raw_pos = export.get("pos", {})
   112→            if isinstance(raw_pos, dict):
   113→                start = raw_pos.get("start", {})
   114→                line = start.get("line", 0) if isinstance(start, dict) else (start if isinstance(start, int) else 0)
   115→            elif isinstance(raw_pos, int):
   116→                line = raw_pos
   117→            else:
   118→                line = 0
   119→            entries.append({"file": norm, "name": name, "line": line, "kind": "type"})
   120→
   121→    logger.debug("knip: %d dead exports", len(entries))
   122→    return entries
   123→
```

1→"""Unused declarations detection via tsc TS6133/TS6192.
     2→
     3→Includes a Deno/edge-functions fallback where `tsc` cannot model URL-based imports.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→import argparse
     9→import json
    10→import logging
    11→import re
    12→import subprocess
    13→from collections import defaultdict
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→
    16→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel, resolve_path, safe_write_text
    17→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    18→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_ts_and_tsx_files
    19→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize, print_table
    20→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors.unused_fallback import (
    21→    _contains_deno_markers,
    22→    _extract_import_names,
    23→    _has_deno_import_syntax,
    24→    _identifier_occurrences,
    25→    detect_unused_fallback,
    26→    should_use_deno_fallback,
    27→)
    28→
    29→TS6133_RE = re.compile(
    30→    r"^(.+)\((\d+),(\d+)\): error TS6133: '(\S+)' is declared but its value is never read\."
    31→)
    32→TS6192_RE = re.compile(
    33→    r"^(.+)\((\d+),(\d+)\): error TS6192: All imports in import declaration are unused\."
    34→)
    35→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    36→
    37→# Compatibility aliases for external callers/tests that imported private names.
    38→_detect_unused_fallback = detect_unused_fallback
    39→_should_use_deno_fallback = should_use_deno_fallback
    40→
    41→
    42→def detect_unused(path: Path, category: str = "all") -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    43→    ts_files = find_ts_and_tsx_files(path)
    44→    total_files = len(ts_files)
    45→    if _should_use_deno_fallback(path, ts_files):
    46→        return _detect_unused_fallback(path, category)
    47→
    48→    tmp_tsconfig = {
    49→        "extends": "./tsconfig.app.json",
    50→        "compilerOptions": {
    51→            "noUnusedLocals": True,
    52→            "noUnusedParameters": True,
    53→        },
    54→    }
    55→    tmp_path = get_project_root() / "tsconfig.desloppify.json"
    56→    try:
    57→        safe_write_text(tmp_path, json.dumps(tmp_tsconfig, indent=2))
    58→        try:
    59→            # Static tsc argv only; no shell expansion and executable path is resolved first.
    60→            result = subprocess.run(
    61→                [
    62→                    _resolve_cli_executable("npx"),
    63→                    "tsc",
    64→                    "--project",
    65→                    str(tmp_path),
    66→                    "--noEmit",
    67→                ],
    68→                capture_output=True,
    69→                text=True,
    70→                cwd=get_project_root(),
    71→                timeout=120,
    72→                shell=False,  # nosec B603
    73→            )
    74→        except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError) as exc:
    75→            logger.debug("Falling back to source-based unused detection: %s", exc)
    76→            return _detect_unused_fallback(path, category)
    77→    finally:
    78→        tmp_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    79→
    80→    entries = []
    81→    for line in result.stdout.splitlines() + result.stderr.splitlines():
    82→        m = TS6133_RE.match(line)
    83→        m2 = TS6192_RE.match(line) if not m else None
    84→        if not m and not m2:
    85→            continue
    86→        if m:
    87→            filepath, lineno, col, name = (
    88→                m.group(1),
    89→                int(m.group(2)),
    90→                int(m.group(3)),
    91→                m.group(4),
    92→            )
    93→            if name.startswith("_"):
    94→                continue
    95→        else:
    96→            filepath, lineno, col = m2.group(1), int(m2.group(2)), int(m2.group(3))
    97→            name = "(entire import)"
    98→
    99→        try:
   100→            full = Path(resolve_path(filepath))
   101→            if not str(full).startswith(str(path.resolve())):
   102→                continue
   103→        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
   104→            logger.debug("Skipping path scope check for %s: %s", filepath, exc)
   105→            continue
   106→
   107→        cat = _categorize_unused(filepath, lineno)
   108→        if category != "all" and cat != category:
   109→            continue
   110→        entries.append(
   111→            {
   112→                "file": filepath,
   113→                "line": lineno,
   114→                "col": col,
   115→                "name": name,
   116→                "category": cat,
   117→            }
   118→        )
   119→    return entries, total_files
   120→
   121→
   122→def _categorize_unused(filepath: str, lineno: int) -> str:
   123→    try:
   124→        p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
   125→        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
   126→        if lineno <= len(lines):
   127→            src_line = lines[lineno - 1].strip()
   128→            if src_line.startswith("import ") or "from '" in src_line or 'from "' in src_line:
   129→                return "imports"
   130→            if src_line.startswith(
   131→                (
   132→                    "const ",
   133→                    "let ",
   134→                    "var ",
   135→                    "export ",
   136→                    "function ",
   137→                    "class ",
   138→                    "type ",
   139→                    "interface ",
   140→                )
   141→            ):
   142→                return "vars"
   143→            for back in range(1, 10):
   144→                idx = lineno - 1 - back
   145→                if idx < 0:
   146→                    break
   147→                prev = lines[idx].strip()
   148→                if prev.startswith("import "):
   149→                    return "imports"
   150→                if not prev or (
   151→                    not prev.startswith("{") and not prev.startswith(",") and "," not in prev
   152→                ):
   153→                    break
   154→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   155→        logger.debug("Unable to read %s for unused categorization: %s", filepath, exc)
   156→        return "imports"
   157→    return "imports"
   158→
   159→
   160→def cmd_unused(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   161→    path = Path(args.path)
   162→    if _should_use_deno_fallback(path, find_ts_and_tsx_files(path)):
   163→        print(
   164→            colorize(
   165→                "Deno/edge TypeScript context detected — using source-based unused scan",
   166→                "dim",
   167→            ),
   168→            file=sys.stderr,
   169→        )
   170→    else:
   171→        print(colorize("Running tsc... (this may take a moment)", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   172→
   173→    entries, _ = detect_unused(path, args.category)
   174→    if args.json:
   175→        print(json.dumps({"count": len(entries), "entries": entries}, indent=2))
   176→        return
   177→
   178→    if not entries:
   179→        print(colorize("No unused declarations found.", "green"))
   180→        return
   181→
   182→    by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
   183→    for entry in entries:
   184→        by_file[entry["file"]].append(entry)
   185→
   186→    by_cat: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   187→    for entry in entries:
   188→        by_cat[entry["category"]] += 1
   189→
   190→    print(
   191→        colorize(
   192→            f"\nUnused declarations: {len(entries)} across {len(by_file)} files\n",
   193→            "bold",
   194→        )
   195→    )
   196→
   197→    print(colorize("By category:", "cyan"))
   198→    for cat, count in sorted(by_cat.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1]):
   199→        print(f"  {cat}: {count}")
   200→    print()
   201→
   202→    print(colorize("Top files:", "cyan"))
   203→    sorted_files = sorted(by_file.items(), key=lambda item: -len(item[1]))
   204→    rows = []
   205→    for filepath, file_entries in sorted_files[: args.top]:
   206→        names = ", ".join(entry["name"] for entry in file_entries[:5])
   207→        if len(file_entries) > 5:
   208→            names += f", ... (+{len(file_entries) - 5})"
   209→        rows.append([rel(filepath), str(len(file_entries)), names])
   210→    print_table(["File", "Count", "Names"], rows, [55, 6, 50])
   211→
   212→
   213→__all__ = [
   214→    "TS6133_RE",
   215→    "TS6192_RE",
   216→    "_categorize_unused",
   217→    "_contains_deno_markers",
   218→    "_detect_unused_fallback",
   219→    "_extract_import_names",
   220→    "_has_deno_import_syntax",
   221→    "_identifier_occurrences",
   222→    "_should_use_deno_fallback",
   223→    "cmd_unused",
   224→    "detect_unused",
   225→]
   226→
```

1→"""State-path and scan-gating helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→import argparse
     5→from pathlib import Path

     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import auto_detect_lang_name
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize

    10→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    11→    scan_inventory_available,

    13→)
    14→
    15→
    16→def _sole_existing_lang_state_file() -> Path | None:
    17→    """Return the only existing language-specific state file, if unambiguous."""
    18→    state_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify"
    19→    if not state_dir.exists():
    20→        return None
    21→    candidates = sorted(path for path in state_dir.glob("state-*.json") if path.is_file())
    22→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    23→        return candidates[0]
    24→    return None
    25→
    26→
    27→def _allow_lang_state_fallback(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    28→    """Whether command can safely fallback to the sole existing lang state file."""
    29→    # Scan should always honor detected/explicit language mapping to avoid cross-lang merges.
    30→    return getattr(args, "command", None) != "scan"
    31→
    32→
    33→def state_path(args: argparse.Namespace) -> Path | None:
    34→    """Get state file path from args, or None for default."""
    35→    path_arg = getattr(args, "state", None)
    36→    if path_arg:
    37→        return Path(path_arg)
    38→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
    39→    if not lang_name:
    40→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
    41→    if lang_name:
    42→        resolved = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / f"state-{lang_name}.json"
    43→        if resolved.exists() or not _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    44→            return resolved
    45→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    46→        if fallback is not None:
    47→            return fallback
    48→        return resolved
    49→
    50→    if _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    51→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    52→        if fallback is not None:
    53→            return fallback
    54→    return None
    55→
    56→
    57→def require_issue_inventory(state: dict) -> bool:
    58→    """Return True when command consumers can rely on the issue inventory."""
    59→    if not scan_inventory_available(state):
    60→        print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    61→        return False
    62→    return True
    63→
    64→
    65→def require_scan_metrics(state: dict) -> bool:
    66→    """Return True when real scan-derived metrics are available."""
    67→    if not scan_metrics_available(state):
    68→        print(colorize("No completed scan metrics yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    69→        return False
    70→    return True
    71→
    72→

    74→    "require_issue_inventory",
    75→    "require_scan_metrics",
    76→    "state_path",
    77→]

    64→def _build_next_payload(

    68→    state: dict,

    75→    )
    76→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    77→    payload["overall_score"] = getattr(scores, "overall", state.get("overall_score"))
    78→    payload["objective_score"] = getattr(

   135→        narrative=narrative,
   136→        plan_data=plan_data,
   137→        command_name=command_name,
   138→    )
   139→    if guardrail_warnings:
   140→        payload["warnings"] = guardrail_warnings
   141→    write_query_fn(payload)
   142→    return payload
   143→
   144→
   145→def _render_empty_queue_view(
   146→    *,
   147→    render_context: QueueRenderContext,
   148→    guardrail_warnings: list[str],
   149→    write_query_fn,
   150→) -> None:
   151→    """Render and persist the empty queue state."""
   152→    strict_score = state_mod.score_snapshot(render_context.state).strict
   153→    plan_start_strict = None
   154→    if render_context.show_plan_context and render_context.plan_for_queue:
   155→        plan_start_strict, _ = _plan_queue_context(
   156→            state=render_context.state,
   157→            plan_data=render_context.plan_for_queue,
   158→            context=render_context.ctx,
   159→        )
   160→    _render_queue_header(render_context.queue, render_context.opts.explain)
   161→    _show_empty_queue(
   162→        render_context.queue,
   163→        strict_score,
   164→        plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   165→        target_strict=render_context.target_strict,
   166→    )
   167→    _write_next_payload(
   168→        queue=render_context.queue,
   169→        items=render_context.items,
   170→        state=render_context.state,
   171→        narrative={},
   172→        plan_data=render_context.plan_data,
   173→        guardrail_warnings=guardrail_warnings,
   174→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   175→        command_name=render_context.command_name,
   176→    )
   177→
   178→
   179→def _render_terminal_queue_view(
   180→    *,
   181→    render_context: QueueRenderContext,
   182→    effective_cluster: str | None,
   183→    show_execution_prompt: bool,
   184→) -> None:
   185→    """Render terminal output for a non-empty queue."""
   186→    dim_scores = render_context.state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   187→    issues_scoped = state_mod.path_scoped_issues(
   188→        render_context.state.get("issues", {}),
   189→        render_context.state.get("scan_path"),
   190→    )
   191→    plan_start_strict = None
   192→    breakdown = None
   193→    if render_context.show_plan_context:
   194→        plan_start_strict, breakdown = _plan_queue_context(
   195→            state=render_context.state,
   196→            plan_data=render_context.plan_for_queue,
   197→            context=render_context.ctx,
   198→        )
   199→    queue_total = breakdown.queue_total if breakdown else 0
   200→
   201→    _render_queue_header(render_context.queue, render_context.opts.explain)
   202→    strict_score = state_mod.score_snapshot(render_context.state).strict
   203→    if _show_empty_queue(
   204→        render_context.queue,
   205→        strict_score,
   206→        plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   207→        target_strict=render_context.target_strict,
   208→    ):
   209→        return
   210→
   211→    potentials = _merge_potentials_safe(render_context.state.get("potentials", {}))
   212→    next_render_mod.render_terminal_items(
   213→        render_context.items,
   214→        dim_scores,
   215→        issues_scoped,
   216→        group=render_context.opts.group,
   217→        explain=render_context.opts.explain,
   218→        potentials=potentials,
   219→        plan=render_context.plan_data,
   220→        cluster_filter=effective_cluster,
   221→    )
   222→    next_nudges_mod.render_single_item_resolution_hint(render_context.items)
   223→    if render_context.show_plan_context:
   224→        next_nudges_mod.render_uncommitted_reminder(render_context.plan_data)
   225→        next_nudges_mod.render_followup_nudges(
   226→            render_context.state,
   227→            dim_scores,
   228→            issues_scoped,
   229→            strict_score=strict_score,
   230→            target_strict_score=render_context.target_strict,
   231→            queue_total=queue_total,
   232→            plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   233→            breakdown=breakdown,
   234→        )
   235→    print()
   236→
   237→    if render_context.items and render_context.plan_data and show_execution_prompt:
   238→        print_user_message(
   239→            "Start working on the task above. When done:"
   240→            " `desloppify plan resolve`. Full queue:"
   241→            " `desloppify plan show`."
   242→        )
   243→
   244→
   245→def _build_and_render_queue_view(
   246→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   247→    state: dict,
   248→    config: dict,
   249→    *,
   250→    resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang,
   251→    load_plan_fn=load_plan,
   252→    build_work_queue_fn=build_work_queue,
   253→    write_query_fn=write_query,
   254→    view: QueueViewConfig,
   255→) -> None:
   256→    """Build queue payload and render output for a queue surface."""
   257→    opts = NextOptions.from_args(args)
   258→    guardrail_warnings = triage_guardrail_messages(state=state)
   259→    target_strict = target_strict_score_from_config(config)
   260→
   261→    plan = load_plan_fn()
   262→    plan_for_queue = plan
   263→    plan_data: dict | None = None
   264→    if view.show_plan_context and (
   265→        plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters")
   266→    ):
   267→        plan_data = plan
   268→
   269→    ctx = queue_context(
   270→        state,
   271→        config=config,
   272→        plan=plan_for_queue,
   273→        target_strict=target_strict,
   274→    )
   275→    effective_cluster = _resolve_cluster_focus(
   276→        plan_for_queue,
   277→        cluster_arg=opts.cluster,
   278→        scope=opts.scope,
   279→    )
   280→
   281→    queue = build_work_queue_fn(
   282→        state,
   283→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   284→            count=None,
   285→            scope=opts.scope,
   286→            status=opts.status,
   287→            include_subjective=True,
   288→            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
   289→            explain=opts.explain,
   290→            include_skipped=opts.include_skipped,
   291→            context=ctx,
   292→        ),
   293→    )
   294→    items = queue.get("items", [])
   295→    if view.collapse_plan_clusters and effective_cluster and plan_for_queue:
   296→        items = filter_cluster_focus(items, plan_for_queue, effective_cluster)
   297→    elif (
   298→        view.collapse_plan_clusters
   299→        and plan_for_queue
   300→        and not effective_cluster
   301→        and not plan_for_queue.get("active_cluster")
   302→    ):
   303→        items = collapse_clusters(items, plan_for_queue)
   304→
   305→    if opts.count:
   306→        items = items[: opts.count]
   307→        queue["items"] = items
   308→        queue["total"] = len(items)
   309→
   310→    render_context = QueueRenderContext(
   311→        queue=queue,
   312→        items=items,
   313→        state=state,
   314→        plan_for_queue=plan_for_queue,
   315→        plan_data=plan_data,
   316→        ctx=ctx,
   317→        target_strict=target_strict,
   318→        opts=opts,
   319→        command_name=view.command_name,
   320→        show_plan_context=view.show_plan_context,
   321→    )
   322→
   323→    if not items:
   324→        _render_empty_queue_view(
   325→            render_context=render_context,
   326→            guardrail_warnings=guardrail_warnings,
   327→            write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   328→        )
   329→        return
   330→
   331→    lang = resolve_lang_fn(args)
   332→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   333→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   334→        state,
   335→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command=view.command_name, plan=plan_data),
   336→    )
   337→    payload = _write_next_payload(
   338→        queue=queue,
   339→        items=items,
   340→        state=state,
   341→        narrative=narrative,
   342→        plan_data=plan_data,
   343→        guardrail_warnings=guardrail_warnings,
   344→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   345→        command_name=view.command_name,
   346→    )
   347→
   348→    if _emit_requested_output(opts, payload, items, command_name=view.command_name):
   349→        return
   350→
   351→    _render_terminal_queue_view(
   352→        render_context=render_context,
   353→        effective_cluster=effective_cluster,
   354→        show_execution_prompt=view.show_execution_prompt,
   355→    )
   356→
   357→
   358→def build_and_render_execution_queue(
   359→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   360→    state: dict,
   361→    config: dict,
   362→    *,
   363→    resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang,
   364→    load_plan_fn=load_plan,
   365→    build_work_queue_fn=build_execution_queue,
   366→    write_query_fn=write_query,
   367→) -> None:
   368→    """Build queue payload and render output for `desloppify next`."""
   369→    _build_and_render_queue_view(
   370→        args,
   371→        state,
   372→        config,
   373→        resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang_fn,
   374→        load_plan_fn=load_plan_fn,
   375→        build_work_queue_fn=build_work_queue_fn,
   376→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   377→        view=QueueViewConfig(
   378→            command_name="next",
   379→            show_plan_context=True,
   380→            collapse_plan_clusters=True,
   381→            show_execution_prompt=True,
   382→        ),
   383→    )
   384→
   385→
   386→def build_and_render_backlog_queue(
   387→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   388→    state: dict,
   389→    config: dict,
   390→    *,
   391→    resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang,
   392→    load_plan_fn=load_plan,
   393→    build_work_queue_fn=build_backlog_queue,
   394→    write_query_fn=write_query,
   395→) -> None:
   396→    """Build queue payload and render output for `desloppify backlog`."""
   397→    _build_and_render_queue_view(
   398→        args,
   399→        state,
   400→        config,
   401→        resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang_fn,
   402→        load_plan_fn=load_plan_fn,
   403→        build_work_queue_fn=build_work_queue_fn,
   404→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   405→        view=QueueViewConfig(
   406→            command_name="backlog",
   407→            show_plan_context=False,
   408→            collapse_plan_clusters=False,
   409→            show_execution_prompt=False,
   410→        ),
   411→    )
   412→
   413→
   414→def build_and_render_queue(
   415→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   416→    state: dict,
   417→    config: dict,
   418→    *,
   419→    resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang,
   420→    load_plan_fn=load_plan,
   421→    build_work_queue_fn=build_execution_queue,
   422→    write_query_fn=write_query,
   423→    command_name: str = "next",
   424→    show_plan_context: bool = True,
   425→    collapse_plan_clusters: bool = True,
   426→    show_execution_prompt: bool = True,
   427→) -> None:
   428→    """Backward-compatible alias for the execution queue flow."""
   429→    if command_name == "backlog":
   430→        _build_and_render_queue_view(
   431→            args,
   432→            state,
   433→            config,
   434→            resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang_fn,
   435→            load_plan_fn=load_plan_fn,
   436→            build_work_queue_fn=build_work_queue_fn,
   437→            write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   438→            view=QueueViewConfig(
   439→                command_name=command_name,
   440→                show_plan_context=show_plan_context,
   441→                collapse_plan_clusters=collapse_plan_clusters,
   442→                show_execution_prompt=show_execution_prompt,
   443→            ),
   444→        )
   445→        return
   446→    build_and_render_execution_queue(
   447→        args,
   448→        state,
   449→        config,
   450→        resolve_lang_fn=resolve_lang_fn,
   451→        load_plan_fn=load_plan_fn,
   452→        build_work_queue_fn=build_work_queue_fn,
   453→        write_query_fn=write_query_fn,
   454→    )
   455→
   456→
   457→__all__ = [
   458→    "QueueRenderContext",
   459→    "QueueViewConfig",
   460→    "build_and_render_backlog_queue",
   461→    "build_and_render_execution_queue",
   462→    "build_and_render_queue",
   463→]
   464→

return bool(open_review_ids_from_state(state or {}))


def cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
    """Return canonical cluster member IDs after plan-load normalization."""
    issue_ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    if not isinstance(issue_ids, list):
        return []
    return [issue_id for issue_id in issue_ids if isinstance(issue_id, str) and issue_id]


def _cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
    """Backward-compatible alias for internal imports."""
    return cluster_issue_ids(cluster)


def plan_review_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
    """Return review/concerns IDs currently represented in queue_order."""
    return [
        fid for fid in _queue_order(plan)
        if not fid.startswith("triage::")
        and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
        and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]


def triage_coverage(
    plan: PlanModel,
    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
) -> tuple[int, int, dict[str, Cluster]]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage.

    When *open_review_ids* is provided, use it as the full set of review
    issues (from state) instead of falling back to queue_order.
    """
    clusters = _cluster_map(plan)
    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    for c in clusters.values():
        all_cluster_ids.update(cluster_issue_ids(c))
    if open_review_ids is not None:
        review_ids = list(open_review_ids)
    else:
        review_ids = plan_review_ids(plan)
    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
```

return bool(open_review_ids_from_state(state or {}))


def cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
    """Return the effective issue IDs for a cluster.

    Some saved triage plans only retain issue membership via
    ``action_steps[*].issue_refs`` after later workflow mutations clear
    ``issue_ids``. Treat those refs as the durable cluster membership so
    coverage and completion can continue without rebuilding the plan.
    """
    ordered: list[str] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()

    def _append(raw_ids: object) -> None:
        if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
            return
        for raw_id in raw_ids:
            if not isinstance(raw_id, str):
                continue
            issue_id = raw_id.strip()
            if not issue_id or issue_id in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(issue_id)
            ordered.append(issue_id)

    _append(cluster.get("issue_ids"))

    steps = cluster.get("action_steps")
    if isinstance(steps, list):
        for step in steps:
            if not isinstance(step, dict):
                continue
            _append(step.get("issue_refs"))

    return ordered


def _cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
    """Backward-compatible alias for internal imports."""
    return cluster_issue_ids(cluster)


def plan_review_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
    """Return review/concerns IDs currently represented in queue_order."""
    return [
        fid for fid in _queue_order(plan)
        if not fid.startswith("triage::")
        and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
        and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
    ]


def triage_coverage(
    plan: PlanModel,
    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
) -> tuple[int, int, dict[str, Cluster]]:
    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage.

    When *open_review_ids* is provided, use it as the full set of review
    issues (from state) instead of falling back to queue_order.

**Committed (2 commits):**

2. **`f97f72a`** — Revert subprocess theater, remove QueueRenderContext, clean dead aliases (41 files, net -12 lines)

**What was cleaned:**
- Removed `_resolve_cli_executable` from 8 files (copy-pasted `which()` wrapper that added nothing)

- Reverted `QueueRenderContext` dataclass back to explicit kwargs
- Removed dead `_cluster_issue_ids` and `_saved_plan_review_ids` aliases
- Renamed `require_completed_scan` → `require_issue_inventory` across the codebase
- Restored `cluster_issue_ids` action_steps fallback that was accidentally lost
- Fixed `repair_state.py` import contract violation

> AGENT

Here's the picture:

**The branch has a clear pattern of churn: 17 "split" commits, followed by 3 "revert" commits that undo some of them, plus 2 "inline" commits.** The net effect is +4500 lines across 203 files (7368 insertions, 2862 deletions).

Here's my honest assessment:

**Legitimate improvements (keep):**
- **State recovery from saved plans** (`recovery.py`, `repair_state.py`, tests) — real feature, ~350 new lines
- **Backlog command** (`backlog/cmd.py`) — real feature, ~45 lines
- **Execution/backlog queue separation** — meaningful architectural split
- **Treesitter compat bridge cleanup** — deleted 5 files, added 2 smaller ones
- **Label dedup, dead code removal** — our recent cleanup

**Concerning patterns:**
- **Split-then-revert churn**: 17 split commits churned ~2000+ lines across dozens of files, then 3 reverts undid ~1000 lines of that. The net result is files grew anyway (e.g. `queue_flow.py` 251→469, `stage_flow_observe_reflect_organize.py` 482→621, `execution_phases.py` 463→619). The splits that weren't reverted added abstraction layers and helper functions that may or may not be justified.
- **Large file growth without reduction elsewhere**: Several files grew 30-60% without corresponding deletions. `queue_flow.py` nearly doubled. These aren't splits (which would shrink originals) — they're net additions of wrapper code, dataclasses, and indirection.
- **Test file growth**: ~1500 lines of new test files is generally good, but some (`test_triage_split_modules_direct.py` at +557) are testing the split boundaries themselves — tests that exist only because of the refactoring.

**Net assessment**: Maybe 30% of the branch's changes are genuinely valuable features/fixes. The remaining 70% is refactoring churn — splits, reverts, re-splits — that left the codebase larger and more layered without clear functional benefit. The "god files" that the splits were supposed to fix mostly grew rather than shrank.

Want me to identify specific remaining over-engineering to revert, or is this enough signal?

   561→+def scan_reconstructed_issue_count(state: StateModel | dict[str, Any]) -> int:

   563→+    if scan_source(state) != "plan_reconstruction":

   565→+    metadata = scan_metadata(state)

   568→+        return max(0, value)
   569→+    return 0

> AGENT

These uncommitted changes break down into 3 categories:

**1. `scan_metadata` simplification (good)**
Removes `inventory_available` and `metrics_available` as stored/required fields — they're now derived from `source` via `scan_source()`, `scan_inventory_available()`, `scan_metrics_available()`. This is a genuine simplification: fewer fields to keep in sync, single source of truth. Also adds `scan_reconstructed_issue_count()` accessor instead of raw dict access. Clean.

**2. Import path cleanup (mixed)**
Replacing `from desloppify import state as state_mod` + `state_mod.save_state(...)` with direct imports like `from desloppify.state_io import save_state`. This tightens imports to the actual module rather than going through the mega-re-export facade. Fine in principle, but it's a lot of churn (status/, helpers/) for a stylistic preference. Not harmful, but not urgent either.

**3. `plan_sync` auto-cluster rebuild after assessment imports (good)**
The `plan_sync.py` change is real functionality: when an import brings new assessments but no new review findings, the auto-clusters should still be rebuilt (e.g., moving from "initial-review" to "stale-review"). The 150-line test covers this specific scenario. Legitimate fix.

**Verdict: These are mostly genuine improvements.** The `scan_metadata` simplification eliminates a real consistency risk. The plan_sync fix addresses a real bug. The import path changes are cosmetic but not over-engineered — they just make imports more direct. No red flags.

"""Shared persistence helpers for command handlers."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from desloppify import state as state_mod
from desloppify.base import config as config_mod
from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError


def save_state_or_exit(state: dict, state_file: Path | None) -> None:
    """Persist state with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    try:
        state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    except OSError as exc:
        raise CommandError(f"could not save state: {exc}") from exc


def save_config_or_exit(config: dict) -> None:
    """Persist config with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    try:
        config_mod.save_config(config)
    except OSError as exc:
        raise CommandError(f"could not save config: {exc}") from exc


__all__ = ["save_config_or_exit", "save_state_or_exit"]
```

   104→def _load_json_object(path: Path, *, label: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   105→    if not path.exists():
   106→        raise CommandError(f"Error: {label} not found: {path}")
   107→    try:
   108→        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
   109→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   110→        raise CommandError(f"Error: failed reading {label}: {exc}") from exc
   111→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):

   113→    return payload

   120→    payload_id = str(payload.get("session_id", "")).strip()
   121→    if payload_id != session_id:
   122→        raise CommandError(
   123→            f"Error: session id mismatch in {path} (expected {session_id}, found {payload_id or '<missing>'}).",

   221→        all_dims.update(ctx.dimension_set)
   222→        combined_cap += ctx.issues_cap

   230→        section += render_historical_focus(batch)
   231→        section += render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)
   232→        batch_sections.append(section)

   234→    if not combined_cap:

   236→
   237→    output_schema = (
   238→        "Output schema:\n"
   239→        "{\n"
   240→        '  "session": {"id": "<preserve from template>", "token": "<preserve from template>"},\n'

   254→        '    "related_files": ["relative/path.py"],\n'
   255→        '    "evidence": ["specific code observation"],\n'
   256→        '    "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",\n'
   257→        '    "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   258→        '    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'

   261→        "  }]\n"

   263→    )

   288→def do_external_start(args, state, lang, *, config: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> None:
   289→    """Start an external review session with CLI-issued provenance context."""

   327→        "status": "open",

   329→        "created_at": _iso_seconds(now),
   330→        "expires_at": _iso_seconds(expires),
   331→        "ttl_hours": ttl_hours,
   332→        "token": token,
   333→        "attest": EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT,
   334→        "packet_path": str(packet_path),
   335→        "blind_packet_path": str(blind_path),
   336→        "packet_sha256": packet_hash,
   337→        "template_path": str(template_path),
   338→        "launch_prompt_path": str(launch_prompt_path),
   339→        "instructions_path": str(instructions_path),
   340→        "expected_output_path": str(output_path),
   341→    }
   342→    safe_write_text(session_path, json.dumps(session_payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   343→    safe_write_text(template_path, json.dumps(template_payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   344→    safe_write_text(
   345→        launch_prompt_path,
   346→        _build_claude_launch_prompt(
   347→            session_id=session_id,
   348→            token=token,
   349→            blind_path=blind_path,
   350→            template_path=template_path,
   351→            output_path=output_path,
   352→            packet=packet,
   353→        )
   354→        + "\n",
   355→    )
   356→
   357→    instructions = "\n".join(
   358→        [
   359→            "# External Blind Review Session",
   360→            "",
   361→            f"Session id: {session_id}",

   407→    print(colorize(f"  4. Optional auto-rescan: `{submit_with_scan_cmd}`", "dim"))
   408→
   409→
   410→def _canonical_external_payload(
   411→    raw_payload: dict[str, Any],
   412→    *,
   413→    session: dict[str, Any],
   414→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   415→    """Return import payload with canonical provenance and required session token."""
   416→    session_meta = raw_payload.get("session")
   417→    if not isinstance(session_meta, dict):
   418→        raise CommandError(
   419→            "Error: external reviewer payload must include top-level `session` object."
   420→            ' Expected: {"session":{"id":"...","token":"..."},"assessments":{...},"issues":[...]}',
   421→        )
   422→
   423→    payload_id = str(session_meta.get("id", "")).strip()
   424→    payload_token = str(session_meta.get("token", "")).strip()
   425→    expected_id = str(session.get("session_id", "")).strip()
   426→    expected_token = str(session.get("token", "")).strip()
   427→    if payload_id != expected_id or payload_token != expected_token:
   428→        raise CommandError(
   429→            "Error: session id/token mismatch in external reviewer payload."
   430→            " Regenerate output using the session template/instructions.",
   431→        )
   432→
   433→    payload = {
   434→        key: value
   435→        for key, value in raw_payload.items()
   436→        if key not in {"session", "provenance"}
   437→    }
   438→    payload["provenance"] = {
   439→        "kind": "blind_review_batch_import",
   440→        "blind": True,
   441→        "runner": str(session.get("runner", "claude")),
   442→        "session_id": str(session.get("session_id", "")),
   443→        "created_at": _iso_seconds(_utc_now()),
   444→        "packet_path": str(session.get("blind_packet_path", "")),
   445→        "packet_sha256": str(session.get("packet_sha256", "")),
   446→    }
   447→    return payload
   448→
   449→
   450→def _ensure_session_open(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   451→    status = str(session.get("status", "open")).strip().lower()
   452→    if status == "open":
   453→        return
   454→    raise CommandError(
   455→        f"Error: session is not open (status={status or 'unknown'}). Start a new session with --external-start.",
   456→    )
   457→
   458→
   459→def _ensure_session_not_expired(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   460→    expires_at = _parse_iso(session.get("expires_at"))
   461→    if expires_at is None:
   462→        raise CommandError(
   463→            "Error: session metadata is missing/invalid expires_at.",
   464→        )
   465→    now = _utc_now()
   466→    if now <= expires_at:
   467→        return
   468→    raise CommandError(
   469→        f"Error: session expired at {session.get('expires_at')}. Start a new session with --external-start.",
   470→    )
   471→
   472→
   473→def do_external_submit(
   474→    *,
   475→    import_file: str,
   476→    session_id: str,
   477→    state: dict,
   478→    lang,
   479→    state_file,
   480→    config: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   481→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   482→    scan_after_import: bool = False,
   483→    scan_path: str = ".",
   484→    dry_run: bool = False,
   485→) -> None:
   486→    """Submit external reviewer output via session, adding canonical provenance."""
   487→    config = config or {}
   488→    session_path, session = _session_payload(session_id)
   489→    _ensure_session_open(session)
   490→    _ensure_session_not_expired(session)
   491→
   492→    if str(session.get("runner", "")).strip().lower() not in _EXTERNAL_SUPPORTED_RUNNERS:
   493→        raise CommandError(
   494→            "Error: only Claude external sessions currently support durable score submit.",
   495→        )
   496→
   497→    issues_path = Path(import_file)
   498→    raw_payload = _load_json_object(issues_path, label="external issues")
   499→    canonical_payload = _canonical_external_payload(raw_payload, session=session)
   500→
   501→    stamp = run_stamp()
   502→    session_dir = session_path.parent
   503→    canonical_path = session_dir / f"canonical_import_{stamp}.json"
   504→    safe_write_text(canonical_path, json.dumps(canonical_payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   505→
   506→    _import_config = ReviewImportConfig(
   507→        config=config,
   508→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   509→        attested_external=True,
   510→        manual_attest=str(session.get("attest", EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT)),
   511→    )
   512→
   513→    if dry_run:
   514→        do_validate_import(
   515→            str(canonical_path),
   516→            lang,
   517→            import_config=_import_config,
   518→        )
   519→        return
   520→
   521→    do_import(
   522→        str(canonical_path),
   523→        state,
   524→        lang,
   525→        state_file,
   526→        import_config=_import_config,
   527→    )
   528→
   529→    submitted_at = _iso_seconds(_utc_now())
   530→    session["status"] = "submitted"
   531→    session["submitted_at"] = submitted_at
   532→    session["submitted_input_file"] = str(issues_path)
   533→    session["submitted_canonical_file"] = str(canonical_path)
   534→    safe_write_text(session_path, json.dumps(session, indent=2) + "\n")
   535→
   536→    if scan_after_import:
   537→        code = run_followup_scan(
   538→            lang_name=lang.name,
   539→            scan_path=scan_path,
   540→            deps=FollowupScanDeps(
   541→                project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
   542→                timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   543→                python_executable=sys.executable,
   544→                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   545→                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   546→                colorize_fn=colorize,
   547→            ),
   548→        )
   549→        if code != 0:
   550→            raise CommandError(f"External review exited with code {code}", exit_code=code)
   551→
   552→
   553→__all__ = ["do_external_start", "do_external_submit", "EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT"]
   554→
```
